Editorial · page 3

EDITORIAL RAMBLINGS

J.G.Coleman Editor

The Mammoth is not extinct!! It has in fact raised another mammoth edition of the Belgrave Bible. Bill Laws in his final fling as Editor dubbed the issue mammoth in the context of its bulk. He modestly failed to mention the mammoth task (not tusk) in the preparation of the tome, and I, because nobody else came forward agreed to try to keep things going and maintain the continuity. Bill has a very professional attitude in the jobs that he undertakes, and The Belgravian was no exception and I only hope that my efforts somewhat match up to his.

This is Belgrave centenary year and a great deal of preparation work has been done by a band of members led by Clive Shippen to make it a real celebration and I am certain that if we all enter into the spirit of things and support all of the promotions to the utmost, it will be a year to remember.

T.C.(Charlie) Jones and I are celebrating our 60th year as Belgravians as also I believe is Norman Dewberry (born 1902) and we have a vast store of memories and can well recall the halcyon days prior to World War II when the Walking Section, the Cross-Country and Road Relay Runners had won all of the National Championships plus many other major events whilst the Track and Field men had, with the exception of a few flashes of success, generally met with moderate success. Now the emphasis has gradually turned and the major successes have gone to the Track and Field men and this year has been stupendous for both Team and Individuals. Details, results for the year are to be found in this issue.

For many years the club had, as compared with today, few summer inter-club committments. With men-only membership we had two annual inter-club fixtures - one with T.V.H. and Ashcombe A.C. for The Doris Shield which was one of the highlights of the season and also a triangular match with Mitcham AC and Croydon A.C. plus inter-club matches with such clubs as L.A.C. and Cambridge Harriers and invitations to the Kinnaird Trophy and Waddilove Bowl, all of these events were more or less limited to 6 or 7 flat events with 2 jumps and sometimes a couple of field events, but now it is reaching saturation point - multi team leagues for male and female - all age groups and whole gambit of track and field disciplines.

We have a large active membership and we usually meet our committments but these very heavy fixtures require considerably more people to organise and officiate. So thats the rub - we have the athletes, but we need many more members to do the official and supernumerary jobs.

The athletic success of the club is one thing, but the financial success is another. Ron Severn our intrepid Treasurer does a super job balancing the accounts, but to perform the miracles demanded of him we need a lot more hard cash, so we earnestly ask you all to support to the utmost, any of the fund raising schemes that will be launched for the club's benefit.

Then to publicity. The club has 3 Press Officers who regularly send reports etc. to up to 10 different newspapers, but the number who take any notice is very small and whilst enhanced reports are agreeable, fair comment is always acceptable, so if anybody out there knows the way in - let's hear from you. Advertising time on television is very expensive to hire, but if all our athletes who are fortunate enough to be appearing at events shown on colour television would wear the Claret & Gold - when at all possible, that would be a bit of free advertising for the Club.

J.G.Coleman Editor

Bill LawsClive ShippenT.C. (Charlie) JonesNorman DewberryRon SevernJ.G. Coleman

Notice · page 4

WHY NOT BECOME A TECHNICAL OFFICIAL?

Our Club is suffering from an acute shortage of technical officials. Our track and field programme each summer covers young athletes, ladies, and men's fixtures in a variety of leagues and other competitions. In every case we have an obligation to provide technical officials.

Why not try your hand at helping in this way?

No previous experience is necessary and you may choose from the pre-season fixture list those meetings at which you are prepared to assist. All judges are only too happy to help newcomers learn the ropes.

The following categories are used in track and field athletics :-

Track Judge Field Judge Starter Marksman Timekeeper

In November each year the Surrey County AAA give an evening's introductory lecture in Wimbledon for newcomers in all these disciplines. If you wish you may take a Grade 4 test the following week, but it is not necessary. All you need is a copy of the A.A.A. rule book, which all officials need to refer to from time to time.

Officiating can be a most rewarding and enjoyable part of the athletics scene. Why not give it a try? For all the advice you need telephone CLIVE SHIPPEN on [phone number withheld].

Clive Shippen

Column · page 5

grapevine

Congratulations to Ann and Guy Sagar on the arrival in February of a baby daughter.

Congratulations to Debbie and Graham Macky on the arrival of a baby daughter - Bridie - born on Christmas Day. Graham ran a 2.16.17 marathon in Hiroshima in October '85 and lowered the club record to 2.16.11 a month later in the Beizing (Peking) marathon. No news yet on whether he's made the New Zealand Commonwealth Games Team.

Graham also writes to say that while in China he met Alan Storey who is spending a year or so out there coaching. Alan said that the Chinese were still talking about the GB U'23 squad's visit last September. Gary and John caused quite a fashion/cultural shock!

Anybody remember P.W.(Jimmy) Green? Jimmy a stalwart of Surrey A.C. since 1924 was the founder of Athletics Weekly. He also became associated with The Bels during, and for a time after, the war when he used the Belgrave Hall facilities for training, as 2nd Claim. He sends greetings to all those he trained with which includes Tom Carter and Charlie Smart. Jimmy is due to have a hip replacement during October and we wish him a quick and good recovery. In with his letter was an unsolicited gift to the Club Centenary Fund for which we sincerely thank him.

Rangit Bhata came to Britain this year as a accredited journalist to cover the Commonwealth Games for an Indian newspaper but owing to the boycott of the Games by India, had to return home early. Here is a copy of a letter he sent to Clive Shippen.

Oxford July 23 '86 Dear Clive,

Today I should have been on my way to Edinburgh to cover the Games for our athletes, but the turn of events has made this a very unhappy trip for me. As I write, I am trying to change my flight so as to go back to work in Delhi, since the whole thing has now gone sour. Hopefully I would be able to plan a proper visit next year, to join all of you in The Centenary Celebrations. It was lovely to meet so many old friends at Belgrave Hall last Saturday. I think there were no two opinions when Don Maclean, Bob Taylor and I agreed that Belgrave Harriers embody what might be regarded as the best aspects of Athletics from every point of view and one hopes very much that the Club would continue to thrive as it has done in the past hundred years. I was also delighted to meet the elders of the Club who had done so much to encourage me in my running, nearly thirty years ago, and thanks to whom I have managed to stay in the world of runners.

Please do convey my warmest regards to all the Bels and assure them that anyone coming to Delhi at any time would be assured of a warm welcome and sufficient running.

Rangit

P.S. Please let me have the schedule of events in order that I am able to plan my 1987 trip, as a runner in the Bels 100 years celebrations.

Brian Pritchard is already working on his 1987 Yacht 3 Miles Handicap. Not only can he be seen hobbling painfully from a recurring knee injury, but we hear that he is due to become a Grandad in the New Year. Congratulations to Jane and Chris.

Kevin Sturman travelled back to the U.K. with his charming wife Anita for a lengthy holiday this summer and raced for the Club whenever possible setting seasons bests of 14.23.13 for the 5000m and 29:48.2 for the 10,000m.

STOP PRESS --- FOR HONEYCHURCH READ HOWELL.. Rosemary and Dave tied the knot on October 4th. Our heartiest Congratulations to you both

Obituary · page 6

IN MEMORIUM: GEORGE E. BENTLEY

IN MEMORIUM

GEORGE E. BENTLEY

Passed away in March 1986, in Dorset aged 67. Joined Bels. 1939. One of a good number of Metropolitan Policemen who have served Belgrave well - brother of the renowned Fred Bentley he walked in many of our club teams and in 1947 he finished 5th in National Junior 10 Miles in which Belgrave finished 2nd team.

Served with RAF during the war as a pilot and attained the rank of Flight Lieutenant. By a coincidence on the boat en-route for Canada for training he met up with brother Fred who, unbeknown to him, was also on his way to Canada to be trained as a navigator.

George was a Grade I Race Walking Judge and on many occasions had been Chief Judge. He retired from the Met. Police in 1980 at the rank of Commander, he was also a Past Chairman of Met. Police Race Walking Club

George E. BentleyFred Bentley
See it on the printed page →
Obituary · page 6

LEN (GINGER) COLEMAN

Died at his home in Londonderry, Northern Ireland on January 26th, 1986 aged 72 years. Joined Belgrave Harriers 1929 as a runner and competed on track, road and cross country - transferred to Race Walking in 1933 and won many "Open" handicaps on the track in the years preceding the war. He was also a member on numerous occasions of our winning Road Walking teams which several times was made up by two pairs of brothers, i.e. Chas. and the late Harry Churcher and himself and brother Joe. In 1938 he dead-heated with brother Joe to win the National Junior 10 Miles (Garret Cup) Championship and with Colin Sutton (who was murdered by terrorists in Malaya) and Reg Elson to win the team title with a record breaking 16 points. Represented Surrey County on Track and Road and gained First Class Standards Medals in AAA 2 and 7 miles Walking Championships.

Served in the Army from 1939 most of the time spent abroad with West African Frontier Forces, was torpedoed off South Africa on way to Burma and returned to W.Africa to complete second tour of duty there. At the cessation of hostilities he was seconded to the Indian Army as catering advisor and remained there until Independance 1947.

On his return to Britain he attempted to get racing fit but too much tropics showed their effects and he finally satisfied himself by jogging with Frank Webb and Norman Dewberry on Wimbledon Common.

He spoke fluent French and when in 1937 the Paris Club U.S.Metro visited us he surprised many club members with his bilinguel prowess when he acted as interpretter. He was a Club Life Member.

Len (Ginger) ColemanChas. ChurcherHarry ChurcherJoe ColemanColin SuttonReg ElsonFrank WebbNorman Dewberry

Obituary · page 6

C.F. (CHARLIE) MUNRO

Passed away in February 1986 at a Nursing Home at Teddington. He was in his early 90s and had been becoming more ill with failing sight and deafness to add to his other discomforts.

Charlie was originally a member of Ashcombe A.C. which later amalgamated with St.Georges Services to become Ashcombe St.Georges A.C. and joined Belgrave Harriers when his old club closed down. He had had a long association with the Bells through out inter-club events at which he was always a keen official and continued to do sterling work in other organisations, such as South of the Thames and Southern Counties Cross Country Assoc. of which he was a Past President until failing health took its toll.

Charlie was cremated at Kingston Cemetary and many club members attended.

C.F. (Charlie) Munro

Obituary · page 6

W.G. (Bill) True

Bill joined the Club during 1935 when he came to Belgrave Hall to give a hand to his pal Charlie Churcher with the decorating, maintenance of the hall. He took up race walking and competed regularly in all club and open events. He served in the Army during the war and after the war resumed competing and was in the scoring team with George Bentley in The Garnet Cup race in 1948. He served on the Club committee for many years and was Club Walking handicaper for about 14 years, was a R.W.A. Grade I Walking Judge and officiated regularly until recently when his health deteriorated. Bill had a droll sense of humour and we will miss him.

W.G. (Bill) TrueCharlie ChurcherGeorge Bentley

Feature · page 7

'Belgrave Centenary Year' W.E.LUCAS D.F.C. PRESIDENT

GORDON BISCOE

'Belgrave Centenary Year' W.E.LUCAS D.F.C. (The 'W' stands for 'BILL') PRESIDENT

It was in the early days of 1936 that Belgrave's spies first reported on a young runner of exceptional promise employed in the Insurance world, and it was Ernie Duffett, one of our past Presidents and Cross Country Secretary for 25 years, who undertook the task of trying to sign him on for Belgrave. This was done with good effect, and Bill Lucas became a member in February 1936.

Bill's first test in Belgrave's colours came within a few weeks of joining, when he ran in the Southern Junior C.C. Championships and finished 32. (The race incidentally being won by Ted Stimpson). This effort resulted in the Belgravian scribe of the time writing, "Lucas, a newcomer ran well in his first important race in our colours and clearly has great potential."

(Cont. p.8.)

THE PRESIDENT - W.E.LUCAS D.F.C.

The following December, in the Club 7m Championship, Bill finished 13th out of 24 finishers and took second prize in the handicap. Also about this time he finished 3rd in the Club Junior 5 miles Championship and scored in a winning Surrey Junior Cross Country team.

In the Spring of 1937, Bill was brought in as a reserve for an Olympic runner, Jim Ginty in the London - Brighton Relay and ran a splendid sixth stage to maintain the lead we then held, and which was only lost in the last few yards at Brighton. It was obvious then that our young runner was destined for the highest class of competition. So he continued to improve, particularly on road and track. His long striding action was not too well suited to the country and his sympathies were not in that direction, but nevertheless they were very fine performances that were recorded in the years that followed.

In 1939 came the war which was to change the course of so many lives. Sgt.Pilot W.E.Lucas became the official title of our young man, who later transferred from fighters to bombers and still later became Pilot Officer and eventually Squadron Leader, a member of the elite Pathfinder Group and a holder of the D.F.C.

Bill was back with the club in 1946 and quickly found his running. He represented Surrey, won a South of the Thames Junior race and figured in a large number of successful teams.

It was in 1948 that Bill reached the peak of his career for, although never having won a Belgrave Championship, a third place in the AAA three mile Championship gained him selection for the Olympic Games 5000 metres held in London. Following the games his running career continued with great personal success until 1954. During those years Bill won Surrey County titles at 1, 3 and 6 miles, represented Great Britain v. U.S.A. and Benelux countries, figured in a winning Southern Counties Cross Country Championship team as well as numerous Surrey County Championship teams and victorious road relay teams. Truly a great athlete, his career was such that it is not possible to itemise it here in detail.

As Bill's active career drew to a close so his administration career began, first coming to the Belgrave committee in 1949 for which we have had his services ever since. Perhaps his first important role was to take over as Cross Country Secretary in 1954 from the man who brought him to Belgrave, Ernie Duffett. This post he held until 1969 when he handed over to Gordon Biscoe. For many years he was honary handicapper and has been of special assistance to our finance sub-committee and at our social gatherings. He was first elected President of Belgrave in 1963 and became a trustee some years later.

Outside of Belgrave Bill's activities have been just as impressive. His voice became known to thousands, possibly millions, as the clearest and most competent announcer at the former White City meetings. In the ensueing years his efforts have earnt him continued recognition. He served Surrey County as a team manager and has at various times been President of:

Surrey County A.A.A. Southern Counties C.C.A. South of the Thames C.C.A. Southern Counties A.A.A. Insurance A.A.A.

and is a Vice-President of the A.A.A.

Now retired, Bill lives happily with his wife Sheena (also a loyal friend and stalwart supporter of Belgrave) in Cowfold, Sussex and keeps in trim by playing golf and tending to his estate. He is of course still active on committees at area and national level and is currently member of a working party studying the possibilities of a single body for amateur athletes. More recently he was rated by the Southern Counties as their nomination for President of the A.A.A. In spite of his activities in the higher echelons of athletes Bill still maintains his Club activities and has been a Track Judge for over 30 years.

As an athlete or administrator, Bill Lucas has never been one to settle for second best, always setting himself high standards and to anyone in athletes who is not prepared to apply himself with similar dedication, Bill's somewhat forthright manner sometimes leaves them in no doubt as to his feelings.

So it is then some fifty years on we may still congratulate outselves for Bill signing in 1936 when now by virtue of his athletic career, administrative ability, personal integrity and competiveness he has been unanimously elected as our Centenary President and the man to lead Belgrave Harriers forward to its next hundred years. We could have no better figure head.

GORDON BISCOE

W.E. (Bill) LucasErnie DuffettTed StimpsonJim GintyGordon BiscoeSheena Lucas

Letter · page 9

From the President.

BILL LUCAS

Belgravians,

In 1987 the Club is 100 years old and on September 25th, 1986 the members were kind enough to elect me as their President to cover the whole of the Centenary Year. This is the ultimate honour which can be bestowed on any Belgravian and I am deeply touched that it should fall upon my shoulders to lead Belgrave into a new era.

This Club has made its mark on athletics in many ways and has lived to survive a whole century during which we have had two World Wars which deprived us of many excellent athletes. In my own opinion, and this I know will be shared by all the older members, our greatest achievements were in the period immediately before and after the last war. We were then predominant in all sections of the sport as shown on our Honours Board proudly displayed at Belgrave Hall. Our track runners were feared; our walkers held sway at all distances and our cross country and road runners devastated their opposition.

It is the natural cycle of events that the predominance of any club should wane from time to time, but Belgrave has been fortunate to remain near the top and now we are enjoying a gradual return to fame by the achievements of our track and field team in rising from Division 5 of the National League to Division 1 in successive years and we all hope that we may win that division in 1987.

We have in the Club many athletes of high calibre backed up by an excellent nucleus of above average performers and it would be my hope that in 1987 we can concentrate our efforts not only to win top track and field honours, but to return to those halcyon days of the 30s and 40s when we won National, Area and County championships on the country and the road. Other clubs manage to raise teams for all these events and it is time Belgrave deposed such clubs as Aldershot Farnham, Birchfield, Gateshead, Thames Valley and Tipton. We have been on the fringe for a number of years, but have failed at the last hurdle. My thoughts go to the Belgrave 12 Stage Road Relay of 1986 which is also the Southern Area Championship - we finished sixth but it would have needed only two of our good athletes who stood by on the sidelines to have won that race and to have gone on to contest the National title.

I have not forgotten the stirling achievements of the Race Walkers who kept Belgrave at the top for a long period of time. I hope they also will concentrate their efforts to make 1987 a bumper year.

To our Juniors Youths and Boys I commend you to dedicate yourselves to your sport - you will be the backbone of our future teams when our present stars are no longer active.

As for the Ladies, I am full of admiration for the progress you have made in the few short years since the section was formed. You have a nucleus of over one hundred members mostly active in some form or another. You also can help to make Belgrave the top athletic club in 1987 and for many years thereafter by selecting a goal and devoting yourselves to its achievement.

We are fortunate in having a first class team of sectional Secretaries, Committee Members and workers behind the scenes who are there to help you all. They blend together with the one object of showing the claret and gold vests to the British public and even to the world. Do not let their efforts go to waste by any lack of support.

As for myself, I will devote my energies towards the same objective and will do everything in my power to assist any member who is in any way in doubt as to his or her future in Belgrave.

Thank you all for your confidence in electing me as your President for the most prestigious year of our long history. We are not the first club to have existed for 100 years, but with a little extra help and devotion from you all we may well become the greatest.

BILL LUCAS

Bill Lucas

Feature · page 10

OUR PATRON Eddie Kulukundis Esq..

WRL

OUR PATRON Eddie Kulukundis Esq.. Early tradition revived as Centenary year approaches

The Patron Tradition When Belgrave was founded 100 years ago competitive athletics was in its infancy and mainly took the form of handicap racing with prizes for the winners. Entry fees went some small way towards meeting costs and it was club patrons who contributed towards the finances needed for the high quality prizes. A Club's prize fund was the most important part of its assets and successful clubs relied heavily on benefactors.

In the early days Belgrave was particularly fortunate in attracting many eminent patrons. No doubt aided by its location in the Belgravia/Westminster area, these included; General Sir H. de Bathe J.P., the Hon. Lord Monk, the Rt. Hon. G. Goschen (M.P. for Westminster), the Rt. Hon. George Cubitt (later to become Lord Ashcombe) and W. R. Burdett Coutts of banking fame. From 1919 to 1939 our patron was Lt. Col. Sir S. Hoare Bt., M.P.

The advent of World War 2 curtailed the activities of many athletic clubs and sadly after 52 years the tradition of patronage came to an end at Belgrave. The growth of athletics from the 1940's onwards saw more emphasis on track and field and road events with prizes often being donated by businesses. More recently we have seen the advent of big money for the very best athletes, the setting up of trust funds, and race promoters rumoured to have bottomless purses for the largest televised meetings.

With these developments the function of a patron is now very different from that of a century ago. A modern day patron is most likely to be a person able to add to the standing of a Club through his or her pre-eminence in the sport.

The relationship between Patron and Club has been likened to a marriage and when the idea of re-establishing an old tradition was first mooted a careful study was made of likely candidates. Eddie Kulukundis' name came top of our list and we were delighted when in November 1985 he accepted our invitation to become Belgrave's Patron as we move into our second Century.

With these developments the function of a patron is now very different from that of a century ago. A modern day patron is most likely to be a person able to add to the standing of a Club through his or her pre-eminence in the sport.

The relationship between Patron and Club has been likened to a marriage and when the idea of re-establishing an old tradition was first mooted a careful study was made of likely candidates. Eddie Kulukundis' name came top of our list and we were delighted when in November 1985 he accepted our invitation to become Belgrave's Patron as we move into our second Century.

Eddie Kulukundis

EDDIE KULUKUNDIS

Who's Who devotes over 40 lines to Eddie and at first glance you would be forgiven for believing that his predominant hobby was the theatre. Chairman and director of several theatrical organisations including the Royal Shakespeare Theatre Trust, Eddie has supported a large number of London productions over the last 17 years. Balancing this interest in the arts is a passion for athletics formally represented by his Directorship of the Sports Aid Foundation, Vice Patron of the AAA's and more recently by his participation in U K team management involving GB Junior teams at European and World Junior Championships. In 1986 he was part of the official U K squad at Athens for the World Junior Championships.

There can't be many members in the Club who have attended every day of every Olympics since 1960. Eddie has (well almost) and in 1984 at Los Angeles he was trackside as part of the Press Corps following his production of a series of coaching films for the IAAF. His knowledge of our sport is considerable and it is amazing how he can recall people and performances from practically any meeting you care to name. It would be a brave man who pitted his knowledge of athletics and athletes at world level against that of Eddie's.

Since 1974 Eddie has quietly provided assistance to literally hundreds of promising young athletes struggling to reach their full potential. Earliest to benefit were Brendan Foster and Steve Ovett and Eddie recognised the needs of their coaches too providing help whenever needed. He has been unofficial patron to the U K junior squads since there early days and the majority of the U K's top juniors over the past 10 years owe a great deal to the practical support and kindness of Eddie Kulukundis.

Eddie tells of how his great enthusiasm for our sport was converted to a practical involvement by 1968 400 m H Olympic gold medallist David Hemery. The occasion was the Munich Olympics when on being congratulated on a bronze medal David could only express his sadness that he had let everyone down. It was David's sportsmanship that kindled Eddie's interest in helping but it was not until the two met again at the 1974 Commonwealth Games in Christchurch that practical plans evolved. From that day Eddie's enthusiasm and help for up and coming Juniors has taken the tangible form of providing financial assistance for those in need. Undoubtedly he has got a great deal of pleasure in seeing so many achieve successes at the highest International level.

Several of our up and coming Juniors have benefitted from his direct support over the last year with warm weather training for our sprinters and support via Mike Winch for our throwers. We hope that the special excitement of being part of Belgrave at this important time will provide a new experience for Eddie and that he will enjoy a greater involvement with the grass roots of our sport. We are certainly proud and honoured that he should have accepted our invitation to be our Patron. We aim to make him proud to be part of us in the years ahead.

WRL

Eddie KulukundisGeneral Sir H. de BatheLord MonkG. GoschenGeorge CubittW. R. Burdett CouttsSir S. HoareBrendan FosterSteve OvettDavid HemeryMike WinchWRL

Report · page 13

The A.G.M.

J.G.C.

The A.G.M.

ANNUAL GENERAL MEETING

The A.G.M. was held at Belgrave Hall on Thursday 25th September 1986 and attended. At the E.G.M. which preceded the meeting the amendment to Rule 5 (b) was carried unanimously with little or no discussion. The main meeting then proceeded with the usual business procedures. Joe Coleman gave the Hon.Gen.Secs. report with particular mention to the necessity for more club officials, particularly in the Track & Field section also for more coaches and the outstanding performances of teams and individuals during the Track season.

Ron Severn rendered his report as Hon. Treasurer and explained many of the facets of the balance sheet which received and accepted with a vote of thanks to him and the two Auditors Ken Miller and Ron Conway. The next business was the election of Ron Severn to the Vice-Presidents list. This was to honour him for services to the club which, although being a Club Member for a few years had given us much considerable assistance using his professional know-how. He was elected unanimously. The meeting then considered the committee proposal that the club subscriptions should be increased to the following scales: Seniors £15, Juniors (u/20) £10, Young Athletes £6, 2nd Claim and Associates £10. Bill Lucas proposed the motion and spoke of the club's real need for increased finances, this was followed by a short discussion and the motion was carried - only one voting against. The new subscription rates take effect as from July 1st 1987.

The election of the Club Officers for 1987 then followed. The committee nomination for W.E.(Bill) Lucas for President was unanimously accepted and after exchanging niceties with outgoing President - Gordon Biscoe - Bill Lucas then took over as Chairman and the other Officers were then elected, viz:

Hon.Gen.Secretary: Joe Coleman Hon. Treasurer: Ron Severn

Section Secretaries: Walking - Carl Lawton Cross Country - Colin Pearson Road Running - Colin Pearson Track & Field - Leo Coy Veterans - Tony Stone Coaching - Bill Laws Official - Derek Crookes

Hon.Sec.Hall & Social: Eric Crutcher Membership Secretary: Carl Lawton

Young Athletes: Cross Country - Reg Hopkins Track & Field - Jim Mote

Ladies: Track & Field - Harry Porter Cross Country - Harry Porter

Trustees: - Tom Carter Frank Webb Bill Couzens Bill Lucas

Hon. Auditors: Ron Conway, Ken Miller

Press Secretaries: Sue Parrot, Geo Piddington, John Wasbrough

Hon.Handicapper: (Running) Arthur Bruce - Hon.Starter: Arthur Penny

Committee: - K.Duckett, Sue Parrot, J.Morris, A.Paton, D.Mote

Hon. Editor Belgravian: - Joe Coleman

The following were elected as Section Captains -

Track & Field - Mark Lindsay-Bailey Cross Country - Dave North Road Running - Olly Foote

Ladies Track & Field - Rosemary Honeychurch Ladies Cross Country - Rosemary Honeychurch Walking Section (Yet to decide)

Any Other Business: The Club Centenary was discussed and Clive Shippen gave details which are announced in more depth in article on other pages of magazine. Bill Couzens reported on both this year's Dinner & Dance and also the Centenary's Grand Dinner & Dance. The meeting closed at 9 p.m. approx. and a large proportion of the attenders retired to the bar to heed the Hon. Treasurer's appeal - I will not put it in writing, but Ron will give you a demonstration anytime, but only at Belgrave Hall Bar, and at Bar hours also bring a container - He will also explain as to why a container!!

J.G.C.

Feature · page 14

PRESIDENTS ELSEWHERE R.W.A. John Keown.

J.W.G.

PRESIDENTS ELSEWHERE

R.W.A. John Keown.

It is with great pleasure that in our Centenary year that the parent body of Race Walking in this country should honour a Belgrave Harrier with its highest office for the ensuing year, thus carrying on a tradition of previous Belgravians like Frank Thomson, Alf Harley, Syd Roberts, John Goswell who held this high office.

John joined the club in August 1954 and although never reaching the top echelon of walkers, turned out to be a first class club man, a true Belgravian, always to be relied on to back up our teams in any event.

Walked his first Belgrave Open November 1955, finishing 122nd out of 200 starters in 67.20. But his true forte was eventually in distance events, and he holds the unique Belgrave record of finishing in twenty-six consecutive London to Brighton Open Walks 1959 to 1984 when it was discontinued owing to traffic and members decreasing problem. He won his first Brighton Winning Team medal in 1961 as fourth club man home in 15th position in 9:26.08, and in the last two years of this Open Walk, has won the Club Long Distance Championship and holder of the Brighton Cup being the only Belgravian to finish. This year he annexed the 'Jimmy Belchamber Cup' and Club Championship in the National 50 Kilometres, being again the only Belgravian to finish in this event.

He gained his Centurion's Badge No.473 in the 100 miles at Ewhurst, Surrey in 1971, finishing in 20 hrs 49mins 27secs and so joined this illustrious band of Walkers, and he has also run and finished in a London Marathon.

Besides his unique racing career, John is a first class administrator. Co-opted to Southern Area R.W.A. Committee when he took over the Editorship of Race Walking Record in December 1962, which he edited until February 1968, he has served on both Area and Main Committees ever since, and was R.W.A. Assistant Secretary 1980-83.

He has been responsible for drafting and amending rules of the Association for many years, was elected Trophies Secretary in February 1978 and is still responsible for all R.W.A. Trophies, attends all Championships and ensures Medals and Trophies are available for presentation, besides walking in most events. He has organised the S.A.R.W.A. Track Walk Competition since its inception in 1972.

Notwithstanding all this, you will still see John selling programmes outside Belgrave Hall before the start of the Open Walk, then at last minute, dash in, change, and then walk in the event. He will assist in any way he can.

A true Belgravian, a credit to the sport, which has brought him an honour he richly deserves.

We wish him well. May he have an enjoyable and great year in office.

J.W.G.

John KeownFrank ThomsonAlf HarleySyd RobertsJohn GoswellJ.W.G.

Feature · page 15

S.T.C.C.A. Derek Crookes.

S.T.C.C.A. Derek Crookes.

Derek must be one of the greatest collector of "jobs".

In the club, which he joined in 1954, he has been Road Running Secretary, Coaching Secretary, General Secretary and President 1981/82. In Surrey County AAA he has been Assistant Secretary, Road Running Secretary and President 1984/85. In the South of the Thames Cross Country Assoc. he has been Sec. for many years and finally this year is President. Other than these "jobs" he finds time literally to act as timekeeper at Track, Road and Cross Country and occasionally Race Walking events. He is a very creditable organiser - and has been responsible for our 20 Miles Road Run for many years likewise in County Athletics he has been a very active worker. He had a very fine record as an athlete and competed in all running events from sprints through middle distance to cross country and road and eventually to marathons. Well done Derek, sorry if any "job" has been overlooked but if rumour has it right, as soon as you got the chain off your neck there's another in the offing. Mary - buy another tin of metal polish!!

Derek CrookesMary

Notice · page 15

RON'S MASTERPLAN

RON SEVERN

RON'S MASTERPLAN

Donations:

Some members of the Club would like to make donations to its funds from time to time, but feel than an amount they could afford would, perhaps, be too little to be worthwhile. This is fully understood. At the last A.G.M. I made a suggestion which all members may like to adopt. I put forward the idea that when a member gets his or her bank statement from the bank, they might send me a cheque, made payable to the Club, for the odd pence in the final balance. Thus, if that balance were, say, £118.76 their cheque would be for 76p. A further suggestion on this is that if they felt able to, their cheque could be for the difference between the balance and the nearest multiple of 5. In the example I have given, the cheque would be for the difference between £120 and £118.76 namely, £1.24p. If, however, the balance were to be, say, £116.50p the cheque would be for the difference between that figure and £115, i.e. £1.50.

These may not seem very large amounts in themselves, but I am sure you will appreciate that if enough members did it, the gain to the Club would be quite substantial. It would also, I hope, be painless for members.

I look forward to receiving hundreds of cheques from you. Don't bother with covering letters. Perhaps I can thank you in advance?

[address withheld]

RON SEVERN Hon. Treasurer

Ron Severn

Column · page 16

From THE BELGRAVIAN ..... 25 years ago .... & 40 years ago ....

From THE BELGRAVIAN .....

25 years ago ....

BELS. RETAIN R.W.A. 50 KILOS

Having narrowly lost two of their three National titles our boys were determined to hold on to this, the last. And hold it they did. For although they were without Bailey, McGreechan and Yescombe they had three men home before any club could finish two. There was no doubt about this team result!

Once more honours go to Ray Middleton who moved up yet again in his National placings finishing second to Olympic Gold Medallist Don Thompson, and setting a new Club record in his first 50 kilos! Ray was the only walker to make Thompson look beatable, and now has placings of 5th, 3rd and 2nd in the year's three premier events. He is surely on the threshold of achieving high honours.

Ray Hall and George Beacham were always well placed and provided the backing for team honours. John Keown walked well to complete our scoring four and was closely followed by D. Bartliff and J. Morris. These three had had a rare battle for 4th place and it was only in the last mile that John asserted his position.

& 40 years ago ....

The first post-war Winter season was a memorable one for the Belgrave Harriers. Our cross-country runners won every open championship. In November we had the first two teams home in the South of the Thames "Junior" and the first three individuals. This in itself was a remarkable feat as all of our pre-war "cracks" cannot run in this race owing to the barring clause. In the New Year we won the Surrey County Title, whilst our Middlesex members, running for Cadogan A.C, took the Middlesex title. This was not regarded as very extraordinary as it had been done on various occasions before the war. The South of the Thames "Senior" Race resulted in another win for us. Here again, a large number of our best men are "barred". The Southern Counties' Cross-Country Championship 7½ miles, instead of the usual 10, was held from Belgrave Hall. Our Captain, Tom Carter, gained a well-earned and popular victory. With David Main third and four others in the first 20 positions, we gained a 99 points margin over the second team. In fact, our team of 12 was home before any other team had finished its six scoring men! Then to the much-awaited English Championship, of which we were the proud holders. On March 9th our lads travelled to Leamington Spa and had another battle royal with our famous Midland rivals, Birchfield Harriers. Our team finished in the first 24 positions, and after a breath-taking adding up of the scores we were acclaimed winners with a ten-points margin. We are indeed truly the Champion Club.

BaileyMcGreechanYescombeRay MiddletonDon ThompsonRay HallGeorge BeachamJohn KeownD. BartliffJ. MorrisTom CarterDavid Main

Feature · page 17

CENTENARY CELEBRATIONS

CS.

CENTENARY CELEBRATIONS

It was on Friday, 7th october 1887, that a group of athletics enthusiasts met in a room at "The Kings Head" Ranelagh Road Pimlico and formally decided to launch a new harrier club. There was much deliberation about the name it should carry. The West of Pimlico was in marked contrast to the high social status of Belgravia to the North and residents in the better parts of Pimlico were wont to call their area South Belgravia. For several reasons, the meeting decided to name the club BELGRAVE HARRIERS.

The catchment area was Chelsea, Battersea, Westminster, Pimlico and Belgravia; an area which included Buckingham Palace itself! Within that area several other clubs existed, for it was a time when Victorian society was witnessing a boom in sporting activity. The clubs waxed and waned with startling frequency. Many died after short lives. Their fortunes depended entirely on the calibre of their officials and their ability to raise money for attractive prize funds.

BELGRAVE HARRIERS suffered many vicissitudes and misfortunes as a club during its early years, but each time there was someone prepared to step into the breach and inject a new lease of life. As other clubs faded and died, Belgrave fought on with a spirit that has never left the club to this day.

Those founder members, seated together in that little room at the King's Head 100 years ago, could never have foreseen where their actions would lead a century later. They could never have imagined that their successors would one day master the giants of that Victorian era - Birchfield, SLH, Finchley and Salford. But master them they did, at one time or another.

The Club has had much to celebrate during its 100 years. National champions at both running and race-walking, and gold, silver and bronze medals for its members in the Olympic, European and Commonwealth Games. As we enter 1987 we rank in the top eight UK clubs with our position in Division 1 of the British Athletics League.

We have a history of which we can be justly proud, and a future of exciting possibilities. We have much to celebrate and a great deal to look forward to. Our Centenary has arrived at the right time!

It is hoped that all of our members, however distant or remote from our activities, will be able to attend at least one of our celebratory functions during 1987. We want to see everyone who has played a part in Club affairs return to the fold during the 12 months of our celebrations. The following diary of events has been planned.

31 Dec/Jan - New Year's Eve Party at Belgrave Hall. Centenary mementoes unveiled and available for purchase. Food, drink, music.

17 January - Cross-country match from Belgrave Hall versus English Cross-Country Union, Surrey County, Middlesex County and other clubs also celebrating their centenaries. After-match reception and buffet.

4 April - Belgrave 12-stage relay (incorporting SCAAA Championships).

11 July - Centenary Club Championships - Battersea Park. Special programme. Fine opportunity for re-unions.

18 July - The 36th Belgrave Open '20' - Souvenir programme.

25 July - River-boat trip on Thames. Food and music. Capacity for 300. Evening function.

7 October - Club birthday (Wednesday). An evening get-together at Belgrave Hall. Un-veiling ceremony.

11 October - Centenary London to Brighton Relay. A special once-only revival of the famous race featuring other centenary clubs and previous winners. Reception in Brighton.

14 November - Centenary Dinner at The Gloucester Hotel, Harrington Gardens, SW.7. South Kensington. Tickets £25 each. Number limited to 200.

28 November - The 68th Open '7' Walk. Special Centenary event. Souvenir programme.

26 December - Fancy dress handicap races on Wimbledon Common with special re-staging of the 1887 120 yards handicap sprint. 11 a.m. start.

31 Dec/1 Jan - Closing New Year's Eve party at Belgrave Hall to "see in" the Club's second century.

It is apparent from the above diary that 1987 is going to be an expensive year for the Club. We are still paying for the recent modernisation of Belgrave Hall and our Treasurer is obliged to keep a tight hold of the purse strings. However, a Centenary fund has been opened which we hope will help to defray the substantial costs of our programme. We therefore appeal to all members to send the Club a Centenary birthday present preferably before the programme commences.

A word of warning about the Centenary Dinner. The number is restricted and the demand could be great. You can reserve your places NOW and help the Club financially if you send in your cash order right away.

1987 is a special year in the Club's history. As a Belgrave Harrier you have much to be proud of and a great deal to celebrate. Do not let it slip by without making an effort to join in.

CS.

CS.

Report · page 19

Hall and Social.

Eric Crutcher

Hall and Social.

HALL AND SOCIAL - Eric Crutcher

Having just completed my first year as Hon. Gen. Sec. (Social) and Hall Manager, there have been times when I wondered how Bill Couzens ran the show single-handed! Certainly without the assistance of Keith Duckett to clean the pipes (which produce immaculate pints), order/collect bar stock and assist on socials when working hours permit, my task would be much more difficult - thank you Keith. I would also like to thank all those members who have served on the bar rota during the year and at the same time appeal for more volunteers now. The duties are not onerous, involving about 3 hours work every 4 weeks or so and, as the accounts show, the bar is an important source of income in helping to fund club activities. In addition, Brian Gorman's efforts in providing the Saturday afternoon refreshments should not go unrecognised and Fred Paget's teas on Sunday mornings are much appreciated.

Looking back briefly over the past year probably the best evening was a darts match between the under 35s and their seniors. Fought over 15 legs the match was perfectly balanced at 7 each going into the final leg where all 8 members of each team played. Tension and excitement were running very high but Mark Sinclair kept a cool head to produce the winning double. We'll certainly have a return match in the winter.

The other notable feature of these last few months has been the reintroduction of suppers at the Hall which have proved very enjoyable. At this point I must say a big thank you to Sue Parrott who, in addition to helping at all the socials, (and officiating at meetings) has been tireless in whipping up support for these supper evenings. This was obviously for the first couple of events, but it was significant that neither Sue of I approached people directly for the October evening (relying instead on 3 posters in the Hall) and attracted less than half the numbers necessary for a successful evening, resulting in cancellation. We have planned more suppers for the winter but it would be much easier on the organisers if people wishing to support the suppers contacted us by the preceding Monday at the latest.

Looking ahead, in addition to the darts challenge and suppers I have fixed dates for a table tennis tournament and a couple of extra darts evenings. The latter events are intended as less serious evenings than the "challenge match" and the Denmark Pot but I hope they will be equally well supported.

In addition we will be holding a pre-Christmas disco and a Valentines Disco and I hope to fit in a "Music of the 60s" evening somewhere along the line!

IMPORTANT NOTICE: NEW YEARS EVE

You will be aware of the plans for a celebration at Belgrave Hall on New Year's Eve to mark the beginning of our centenary year. There will be live music (not a deafening disco!), a buffet and champagne up until 1 am. The centenary draw will be made during the evening for which you have already been asked to sell some tickets and may I ask you again to make every effort to raise money through this draw to help finance our special centenary events.

Admission to the New Years Eve celebrations will be by ticket only. Although this does not sound very satisfactory for such a momentous occasion I am sure you will appreciate that with a Hall capacity of 140 and a club membership of over 700 it is unlikely that we would be able to accommodate everyone that would wish to be there. If we were to allow entrance to more than 140 then the Hall becomes overcrowded, uncomfortable and consequently unfair on those who booked their tickets well in advance. At the time of writing (23 October) we already have bookings for 70 tickets, so please ring Sue Parrott ([phone number withheld]) or myself ([phone number withheld]) as soon as possible to make sure of your place. YOU WILL NOT BE ALLOWED ENTRANCE WITHOUT A TICKET.

I should be grateful if those who have already indicated that they wish to attend, forward payment for their tickets by early December please.

LIST OF SOCIALS (all events start 8.00 pm)

These events will only be successful if there is a good response from you.

Please make every effort to support the club, especially in its Centenary year. With best wishes.

1987 LONDON MARATHON

I have applied for an allocation of places in the 1987 London Marathon out of the 2,000 reserved for club runners who receive rejection slips. If you are one of those unfortunates but would still like to run, then please forward your rejection slip to the Hall as soon as possible. If there are not enough places then a draw will be held during early December.

ERIC CRUTCHER

Notice · page 20

MEMBERSHIP AND SUBSCRIPTIONS

MEMBERSHIP AND SUBSCRIPTIONS

Stand by for blasting - Carl Lawton, our "Membership" Hon. Sec. has a few well chosen words to impart.

Quote - "As with most official jobs in Belgrave Harriers, the Membership Hon. Secretary's job is consuming of time, but it is often made easier by those who reply promptly to the first renewal reminders.

Unfortunately we have those who would rather wait until the second reminder and then those who ignore that as well with the air that "I should not need to pay". Last year we had cases where "Recorded Delivery" letters were sent and even those were ignored and yet some of these athletes decided to turn out a few months later to compete as if nothing was amiss having recently paid or had their subs paid for them.

Those of you who attended the A.G.M. will have heard that new members of approximately 120 was only slightly higher than those who had resigned or had been deleted. There is no doubt that we should not be losing so many and maybe in future we should endeavour to hold on to more of those who tend to disappear from the scene.

My thanks to those of you who generously give donations or add donations to their subscriptions which help to boost our much needed funds." Unquote

Carl Lawton

Report · page 21

Indoor Round Up

Steve Williams

Indoor Round Up.

INDOOR ROUND UP

Once again we can record successes for our men who competed indoors this Winter, with International victories, AAA's titles, World best and club best performances. The following is a brief round up of the details.

Arrow Cosford Games - 10th/11th January: Having sought an English qualification through his many years residency in this country (following his declaration never to compete again for Ghana) Ernie Obeng set about impressing his name on the selectors for the later series of dual internationals with a fine win over Sharp and Bunney in 6.71. Ken Onuaguluchi made his indoor debut with 6th place in the shot with 14.79. * * * AAA Indoor Championships - 24th/25th January: Times were again proved to be irrelevant on the Cosford 60m surface as Ernie recaptured the title he won two years ago, holding off the man who was to go on to win the European Indoor Championship. (1st E Obeng 6.72, 2nd R Desruelles 6.73, 3rd C Sharp 6.75). Marcus Adam (6.95) and new member Errol Roofe (7.00) just failed to qualify for the final. In the 200m final, John Regis did well to run 21.82 from the inside lane, just missing a medal, following a very fine 21.56 semi final (club best performance). Marcus Adam recorded a 22.40 heat but could not progress from the semis, another victim of the inside lane. Derrick Williams made a rare indoor appearance long jumping 6.88 for 9th place and Ken Onuaguluchi threw a hefty 15.42 shot for 6th place (our outdoor record is 15.78!) * * * Spain v UK - Madrid - 1st February: John Regis was one of only two mens individual winners for the UK in this International, mastering the 164 metre track to such an extent that the best home runner was fully 2/10ths behind. 1st J Regis 21.71 2nd Prado 21.91. Ernie Obeng in West Germany: 26 Jan, Sindelfingen 6th 6.85(6.79ht); 31 Jan, Karlsruhe 4th 6.75; 2 Feb, Stuttgart 5th 6.77(6.75ht).* * * UK v Hungary - Cosford - 8 February: Ernie Obeng's efforts had not gone unnoticed and a new international career began with him blasting to victory by just about as big a margin as possible over 60m in an International event. 1st E Obeng 6.71, 2nd Tartar 6.81.* * * UK v USSR - Cosford - 25 February: Ernie had to bow to the Soviet, Yushmanov, after not the best of starts but finished well to take second in 6.77. John Regis placed 3rd in the 200m with 21.92, again from that inside lane.* * * UK v USA - Cosford - 8 March: The Olympian, Thomas Jefferson proved too strong for Ernie over the last 20 metres but Ernie confirmed his position as the domestic number one in a time of 6.73.* * * British Veterans Championships-Cosford 15 March: Snowy Brooks continues to make his mark in the vets ranks, scoring a World Best total of 2749 points in the Pentathlon, including an equal World Best time for 60m Hurdles of 9.3. Snowy's other marks were 5.77(LJ) 11.74(SP) 1.63(HJ) and 3.12.2(1000m) and he also took a bronze medal in the individual 60mH.*** AAA's Under 20 Championships - Cosford - 21\22 March: Bels had the 200m all sewn up with Marcus Adam holding off Phil Goedluck 22.02 to 22.04 for Gold and Silver, for what must be a unique one-two from youngsters in the same club. Phil also took 4th in the 60m in 6.94(6.89ht) with Marcus unfortunately disqualified in the heats for false starts.

Errata: In the last Belgravian, I referred to John Regis still being a Junior in 1986; well that turns out to be a piece of duff gen for although John is only 19 through the Summer his 20th birthday falls before the end of the year. Apologies too to Marcus Adam (without the s) for getting his name wrong previously.(At least Marcus doesn't have to put up with such variations as Ongulchi, Onagalchi or Onugulachi like poor old Ken 'the typists nightmare'!) Also, a friend of mine wished me to point out that A Minter, whose name appeared alongside a time of 4.31.4 on page 56 was in fact A Painter.

Steve Williams

Report · page 22

Race Walking

Carl Lawton

Race Walking.

WALKING REPORT from Carl Lawton

14-15th SEPTEMBER 1985 COLCHESTER 100KMS/100M/24 HOURS

Rain and wind greeted competitors, officials and attendants for this British Telecom sponsored meeting on the Colchester Garrison track. Of the 35 entries only 3 failed to start. Charlie Weston (Highgate), Ed Shillabeer (Dawlish) and Bob Dobson (Southend) were the early leaders. As the hours progressed Ed Shillabeer went into a lead to win and retire at 100kms with a new British record. Bob Dobson went through to take the 12hr record before taking a break, this left Charlie Weston to go for the 100 miles and finally Peter Worth to cover the greatest distance at 24 hrs. Belgrave, however, had no wins or records, once again it was left to John Morris and John Keown to fly the colours, John Keown was only going for 100 kms and John, 'there's no way you can't finish a 24 hour' Morris retired hurt at 75 miles.

100Kms: 1.E.Shillabeer (Dawlish) 9:41.54; 2.R.Dobson (Ilford) 9:56.34; 3.C.Weston (Highgate) 11:10.05; 9.J.Keown (Bels) 12:35.16; 17.J.Morris (Bels) 13:17.36.

A lesson to be learned from this event is that one must have adequate recorders, with only one person taking laps for 32 competitors and 1 or two others tabulating all the laps covered and a heavy gust of wind thrown in to distribute laps sheets in all directions there is little need to say that there were problems.

9th NOVEMBER 1985 ENFIELD OPEN 7 MILES

There were days when Belgrave could turn up at any race and it would be asked "which Belgrave team will win" now it is slightly different, we can still win, but not always convincingly. 23rd NOVEMBER 1985 WIMBLEDON 66TH BELGRAVE OPEN 7 MILES

To hand at the time of production is the result of the 35th Belgrave 7 published in the December issue of the Race Walking Record in 1954. It makes interesting reading, winner was Len Evans of Highgate in 54.53. Further down the field could be found 23rd C. Ball (Soton) he was his Club's Secretary then and still is now. In 33rd and 36th positions were the late Harold Whitlock and the late Kew Neilson, 31 years on they were to leave us within days of each other, both men were in their ways an example to us all. Further down the field hidden amongst the 177 finishers was a certain D. Fotheringham in 117th place in a shoddy 66.39, he won't stay in the sport long with that sort of time. Well maybe some one was proved wrong for 31 years on Doug got around the course, maybe not exactly the same one but still over distance, FIVE minutes faster and has been the menace of the results typist ever since as his name usually managed to encroach into the next column. Incidentally there appeared to be a total of 26 disqualifications or retirements, a far cry from today with maybe one or two, are we walking that better or are the judges too lenient??

Anyway moving onto this year, won comfortable by Les Morton from Sheffield. Jimmy Ball (son of the aforementioned Cliff who finished 50th this year) was 2nd to be in the frame for the 4th consecutive year, and 3rd also from Sheffield Ray Hankin. Belgrave's first man home was Paul Warburton confirming his previous performance this year in 5th, although beaten by Steyning youngster Darren Stone. The team race was another win for Belgrave, but only due to Stone not being entered in the Steyning team, John Llewellin replaced Eric Crutcher as 4th man and John Hall and Carl Lawton repeated their Enfield positions. On the negative side one should ask whether a team with an average age of 36½ should even be a team let alone a winning team.

Thanks as always must go again to those that make the Belgrave 7 a success. The officials, timekeepers, pointsmen (we could do with more of those) recorders, judges and of course Jack and Joan Goswell, and the others I have forgotten, AND those that donate to the prize fund.

Results:

1.L.Morton (Sheffield) 50.35; 2.J.Ball (Soton) 51.21; 3.R.Hankin (Sheffield) 52.19; 5.P.Warburton 53.18; 8.C.Lawton 54.39; 10.J.Hall 55.02; 18.J.Llewellin 56.53; 22.E.Crutcher 57.46; 30.R.Dorman 60.14; 34.D.Fotheringham 61.26; 38.J.Dunsford 62.47; 49.D.Sharpe 65.16; 66.R.Day 70.23; 68.J.Keown 70.44; 70.J.Morris 72.34;

Team: 1.BELGRAVE 41; 2.Steyning 48; 3.Sheffield 59.

12th JULY 1986 ENFIELD NATIONAL 50KMS

John Keown first competed in a National 50Kms in 1960 and has competed in another 24 since then, apart from two years when he failed to finish this year was his slowest time just inside 6 hours but that was sufficient for him to win the Club Championship for the first time, being the only Belgrave finisher. Although congratulations are in order, John will be the first to admit that it is no credit to the club in only finishing one man. One would like to think that we have reached the bottom and can only get better.

The race as a whole tough was not exactly a class field. The complaint a few years ago was that we must have short lap circuits and IAAF rules, in order to get fast times. The end result is that the race is won by a Belgium by 13 minutes none of the British 50kms 'fast men' start and the 5 of the first eight places are filled by veterans or near veterans.

Results: 1.G.DeJonckere (Belgium) 4:10.21; 2.C.Berwick (Leic) 4:23.22; 3.A.Trigg (Leic) 4:25.29; 45.J.Keown 5:59.44.

Team: 1.Leicester 28; 2.Coventry 62; 3.Royal Sutton 77.

Left: Murray Day,N.Z.Commonwealth Games Representative, Winner R.W.A. 10K Track and 2nd AAA 3K Champs.

Above: John Hall, Eric Crutcher, Paul Warburton, Carl Lawton early in the Club 10M Champs.

Report · page 29

Young Athletes

IAN HORTON

Young Athletes.

We started the '86 - '86 Cross Country season concerned after learning that a number of key runners had departed and coupled with a distinct fall of new membership, we entered the season with a smaller band and greater trepidation - "would we be able to improve on the overall successes achieved during the '84 - '85 winter?"

"Young Bels shine bright" 18.11.85 "Bel boys keep top spot" 28.12.85 "Belgrave youngsters show way home" 2.2.86 "Bels show style" 14.2.86 "Tropy treat for young Harriers" 7.3.86 "Bel boys get taste of real glory" 20.3.86

This selection of newspaper headlines confirm that we need not have been so concerned, illustrating a consistent flow of success throughout the season and providing heartening reading for the increasing band of supporters. Belgrave readers would have noted that we were closing our "five year program" and therefore would not be surprised by my decision to stand down from the Team Manager's role in order that I may concentrate on coaching in the future. There is no doubt that I am leaving "at the top" and handing over a squad which again has achieved results beyond most expectations.

Credit must go to the young athletes who have regularly ran and discovered the basis of all over successes. I must thank all the helpers who have contributed to supporting the teams and a special thanks to Rosemary and Claire for yet again 100% attendance and 110% effort in maintaining personal interest in each and every performance. Just another ingredient of our collective successes.

"To notice a lad, struggling to finish but succeeding, or steadily moving through the field week after week gave as much satisfaction as any other performance," underlining our philosophy that in cross country everybody can be a winner! I am afraid, therefore, that the young athletes have not seen the last of me!

Good Luck and more successes to everybody this winter.

IAN HORTON

Ian HortonRosemaryClaire

Report · page 30

COLTS

COLTS:

Probably the most frustrating team performances all season was from this section, when various external circumstances only allowed half the 'A' team to run in the Downs and the other half at Herne Hill. Their quality showed in that they convincingly won the Downs League (for the second year) and finished seventh in the strong nerne Hill League. Even so it was not surprising, therefore, that when they all came together in the Surrey Championship, the essential spark was missing and the team consequently finished below the undoubted talent that it possessed.

Throughout the season there was some outstanding individual performances from Ed Bojtler, Baber Khan, Alexander Stevens, Dan Winson and Neil Webster and not least, Steven Fox, one of the boys who steadily moved through the field.

BOYS

BOYS

The Boys produced some sparkling performances to take the Downs League again for the second consecutive year. Always in support to combine with the Youths to take the Surrey League Title (a coverted award) and not overawed in the company of Juby (National Champion), to finish a creditable 7th in the Herne Hill League. One of our fast improving athletes, Saul Herbert finishing in the top ten overall.

Well done also Aaron Bacon and Matthew Egerickx for moving into this age group with such obvious success.

Report · page 33

YOUTHS

YOUTHS

This squad again achieved the high performance that the Club has almost come to expect. Crushing all opposition in the Surrey Road Relay they proceeded to equally dominate the Surrey Cross Country Championships, lifting the Camberley open en route! Outstanding individual performances meant a team third in the Southern Championships (a Club first). They combined with the Boys to take the Surrey League Trophy for the first time and rounded off the season with a close second to the very strong G.E.C. in the Herne Hill League, somewhere they also found the time to win on the Downs.

Much of this success should be credited to Mat Kinane, finishing as the individual overall winner of the Herne Hill League, consistently shadowed all season by Gary McGurren, taking 3rd place overall against such strong opposition - these were remarkable performances. Adam Horton finished second overall in the Surrey League and made the major contribution in lifting that trophy, Matthew Smith was always present, always completing a team. Steve Webster pulled off some fine runs, studies permitting! While exceptional quality performances were provided by Glyn Tiernan not least in the Southern.

Well done on an outstanding season!

Report · page 34

YOUNG ATHLETES SECTION REPORT - SUMMER SEASON 1986

JIM MOTE

YOUNG ATHLETES SECTION REPORT - SUMMER SEASON 1986

Young Athletes League

5th MAY 1986

The season opened on a fine afternoon at Tooting Bec where we met the team which was eventually the league champions (Tonbridge). Against their overwhelming numbers our track athletes performed extremely well, but once again we had many gaps in our field team, especially amongst the throwers. In the end we finished second behind Tonbridge but well in front of the third team - Horsham.

Results: Belgrave Winners

Youths: G.Allison HJ 'A' 1.70m; 500mH: 'A' 61.5; 100mH: 'A' 15.6. J.Coy 400mH 'B' 69.2; J.Arnick 400m 'A' 55.5; A.Grant 100mH 'B' 16.5 M.French 100m 'A' 12.0. M.Lawrence LJ 'B' 4.95; N.Khan Discus 'B' 5.06

Boys: R.Affleck TJ 'A' 9.80, 400m 'B' 70.1; S.Bird 80mH 'A' 15.0. M.Bowers 800m 'A' 2:21.6; M.Egericx 800m 'B' 2.28.8.

Relays: 4x400m Boys 4:33.4; 4x100m Youths 48.6; 4x400m Youths 3:39.6.

Team: Tonbridge 261; BELGRAVE 160; Hersham 43.

25th MAY 1986

Our second meeting at Erith track coincided with the "Fun Run" organised by Bob Geldorf in Hyde Park and resulted in a distinct lack of middle and long distance runners in all teams. Our coach to the track was only half full, but on this occasion with the help of our new throwers from Mike Smalls school, we managed to pull out all the stops and win the day.

Results: Belgrave winners -

Youths: G.Allison 400mH 'A' 60.7, HJ 'A' 1.65, 100mH 'A' 15.6; James French 200m 'B' 26.1, 100m 'B' 12.6; N.Sutehall Shot 'B' 5.77, 400m 'B' 64.6; A.Grant 100mH 'B' 16.9; J.Smith 800m 'A' 2:14.0.

Boys: G.Dorrell Hammer 'A' 16.66; P.Dunn Hammer 'B' 11.66; A.Ross 200m 'A' 26.7; 400m 'A' 58.8, LJ 'B' 4.56; A.Stephens 80mH 'B' 16.7

Colts: N.Webster 1500m 'A' 5:17.6. Stew Booth 100m 'B' 16.6

Relays: 4x400m Boys 4.17.2; 4x100m Youths 49.1; 4x400m Youths 3:56.3.

Team: BELGRAVE 153; Hercules W. 141; Kingston P. 105; Ashford 64.

8th JUNE 1986

Back to Erith again for our third meeting, but again with a depleted team this time for a variety of different reasons. We had to settle for third place behind Bexley and East Kent. Our athletes competed with determination and vigour but in the end we were outnumbered and outscored by the two local teams.

Team: Bexley East Kent BELGRAVE

29th JUNE 1986

Our home match at Battersea opened in some confusion, but this time it was our officials team that was depleted. Our athletes took advantage of "playing at home" and fully deserved to win the meeting. This victory showed that when we can field a team anywhere near our best in numbers we are a match for anyone.

Results: Belgrave Winners were -

Youths: G.Allison HJ 'A' 1.65, 400mH 'A' 59.7; J.Coy Hammer 'A' 13.54, 400mH 'B' 70.5; N.Khan TJ 'A' 8.46; M.French 100m 'A', 200m 'A' 24.5; J.French 100m 'B' 200m 'B' 24.5; A.Grant 400m 'A' 56.7; K.Beisty 400m 'B' 57.8; S.Warr Shot 'A' 8.42 Jav. 'A' 32.80; S.Herbert 1500 S/C 'A' 5:24.6; K.Gareeb LJ 'A' 4.97

Boys: G.Darrell Hammer 'A' 20.54; P.Dunn Hammer 'B' 14.44, Jav. 'B' 15.84; I.Gilley 100m 'A' 12.1, 200m 'A' 24.9, L.J. 'A' 5.99; J.Harris 80mH 'A' 14.5; R.Affleck L.J. 'B' 5.03

Colts: S.Abbeysinger 800m 'A' 2:29.3; S.Booth 100m 'B' 15.8.

Relays: 4x100m Boys 52.00; 4x100m Youths 48.1; 4x400m Youths

Team: BELGRAVE 183; Hastings 138; Herne Hill 131

27th JULY 1986

Back to Tooting for our final meeting full of hope only to meet with disappointment and finish third. Holidays, injuries and illness hit us this time but the team performed again with great enthusiasm and with a little luck might have snatched second. In the end it was not to be and so ended a season which promised much and had some highlights accompanied by disappointment.

Results: Belgrave winners were -

Youths: G.Allison 400mH 'A' 59.3, H.J 'A' 1.65, 400m 'B' 57.6; M.French 200m 'B' 24.3, 100m 'A' 11.9; J.French 100m 'B' 12.0; K.Beisty 400m 'A' 56.8; W.Olufunwa H.J. 'B' 1.60, 100mH 'A' 16.7;

Boys: I.Gilley 100m 'A' 12.0, 200m 'A' 23.9, L.J. 'A' 5.98; G.Dorrell Hammer 'A' 20.0; S.Affleck T.J.'B' 8.31; S.Bird 80mH 'A' 14.1; A.Ross 80mH 'B' 14.6, 100m 'B' 12.7 S.Fox H.J. 'A' 1.20; R.Affleck L.J. 'B' 4.93.

Colts: M.Burnett 200m 'A' 28.0; B.Khan 1500m 'B' 5.19.9

Relays: 4 x 400m Boys 4:09.9; 4 x 100m Youths 47.4.

At the end of the competition Belgrave finished seventh out of fifteen clubs which is approximately the same position as last year. Our problem was always availability, but everyone competed when they could and the camaraderie and good fellowship that developed through the season made it all worthwhile.

Two mentions I would like to make concern Matthew and John French who were ever-present and amassed a vast total of points with their wins and second places in the Youths sprints (first year youths at that!!) and Gareth Allison who missed just one match when his school needed his presence at another competition, but who whilst competing for Belgrave in the Young Athletes League this year was never beaten. A fine record when one considers his range of events included 400m Hurdles, 100m Hurdles, High Jump and 400m flat.

Finally, we all enjoyed watching and cheering Ian Gilley who was with us again after a break of a year and we hope he will join us again in future.

HAC MEMORIAL TROPHY 3rd JULY 1986

We relinquished our hold on this Trophy which we won in such great style this year. We finished fourth out of seven clubs and the worthy winners were Garth School.

THAMESIDE LEAGUE

This competition continues to flourish, although with some notable exceptions it was not well supported by members of our section. For a change all meetings were held in good weather (just) and I extend my thanks to all those officials and parents from Belgrave and other competing clubs who worked so hard to make these meetings the success that they were. How the officials manage to incorporate so many non-scoring events and keep so close to the time table is a marvel to me. Well done!!

ROSENHEIM LEAGUE

At the time of drafting this report I have learned that our Youths have qualified for the final tie of this competition to be held on 20th August 1986 at Tooting Bec. It is no secret that our youth team in this competition has consisted of John Coy and one or two others. John seems to have made in his personal goal to get the youth team into the final and he has done it!! John has run, thrown and jumped at every meeting and at a conservative estimate must of scored 75% of all the points credited to the team. I hope we are able to give him the support he deserves on the final night.

It has been an interesting and challenging season in which we have all made many new friends. Keep fit and come back again next year and we will do it all over again.

JIM MOTE

JUNIORS STRIKE GOLD

4th JANUARY 1986 FRIMLEY SURREY COUNTY CROSS COUNTRY CHAMPIONSHIP

Over a rugged and frozen course there was only one team - The Juniors. Andy North took the race by the scruff of the neck from the gun and would have created a record on Hughie Green's Clapometer at the finish line. Andy North was determined to run the finish out of Nick Hopkins, his main rival and took off very fast with only Richard O'Hara taking up the chase. Andy's spikes went missing five minutes before the start and he borrowed a pair from Adrian Ross. Andy was about twenty seconds up at the end of the first lap and had increased this to twenty eight at the finish, in what was a very fine performance. Behind him his Belgrave team mates were also determined to win the team prize. Graham Adams was a fine fifth and was awarded with a Surrey Vest Richard O'Hara was a really good tenth with Simon Maynard closing in the team in twelfth. Spare a thought for Andy Moore who had his best ever C.C. run in fifteenth place and went home unrewarded.

Juniors: 1.A.North (Bel) 25.12; 2.D.Cowan (Boxhill) 25.40; 3.N.Hopkins (Reigate) 25.50;

BELS: 5.G.Adams 26.43; 10.R.O'Hara 27.23; 12.S.Maynard 27.34; 15.A.Moore 27.52; 32.A.Bailey 30.42;

Teams: 1.BELGRAVE 23; 2.AFD 38; 3.Reigate 48.

8th FEBRUARY 1986 STANMER PARK, BRIGHTON SOUTHERN CROSS COUNTRY CHAMPIONSHIP

Juniors Shine Again

On a bright day and a snow covered Stanmer Park course hopes were high that Andy North would win his first major title. Andy was hoping that the bout of Chicken Pox a few weeks earlier would not have any effect. The Surrey Champs. were also determined to give Tonbridge a fight for the team prize. At the end of the first lap Andy was in fifth place not looking too good, but hanging on gamely. Behind him Simon and Graham were working their way through to good positions with Matt Chaston and Richard O'Hara fighting to complete the scoring team. John Booth had these two in his sights and was ready to fill any gaps in the team.

Andy fought bravely but admitted afterwards that he was weakened by his illness. Simon had a brilliant run to finish 20th. Graham steadily picked up places and ended up in 39th place with Matt Chaston also coming through late to finish a very fine 48th. Richard blew out and finished back in the 70s with 'JB' not far behind. Tonbridge had a fine team win, but we finished a really good 2nd.

Results:

Juniors: 1.S.Newport (Tonbridge) 33.18; 2.P.Starling (Southampton) 33.15; 3.N.Hopkins (Reigate) 33.33.

BELS: 8.A.North 34.19; 20.S.Maynard 34.59; 39.G.Adams 36.01; 48.M.Chaston 36.14; 73.R.O'Hara 83.J.Booth

Teams: 1.Blackheath 2.Basildon 3.Brighton

1st MARCH 1986 NEWCASTLE NATIONAL C.C. CHAMPIONSHIPS

Junior:

Andy North was fighting fit and determined to make up for his disappointing Southern. The course was snow covered with a nasty hill towards the end of the lap. Bels were drawn well on the inside and were surprised to find that Tonbridge had pulled a flanker and were lined up alongside. Simon and Andy were away well and Simon led the field after half a mile. At the mile Andy was making the race with Simon about twelfth and Graham and Matt in the early fifties. A team medal of some colour was definitely on at this stage.

At the end of the first lap, Andy was just adrift of the first three, but was still going really well. Behind him the remaining Bels were slowly slipping backwards and we all thought that we had blown a team medal. Andy finished a fine 6th, one of best ever runs by a Belgrave Junior, and surprise, surprise our fine team was rewarded with a Bronze Medal, behind Tonbridge and Birmingham Univ. A historic day for Belgrave and a great reward to a really fine team.

Andy North gets Senior Vest

A week after the National C.C. Champs., Junior Andy North received the great honour of being invited to run in a Senior E.C.C.U. representative match at R.A.F. Halton. Matched against the best of strong Combined Services and British University teams he finished a very creditable 15th out of a 45 strong field. In the process of which he beat Plassy, running also for E.C.C.U. who had finished 26th in the previous week's Senior National C.C. Champs. race.

15th FEBRUARY 1986 COULSDON SURREY LEAGUE - DIVISION I

RELEGATION LOOMING?

We were 6th out of the seven teams in the League and in line for relegation. Croydon were 32 pts ahead of us and so we had it all to do in this final match. With Harry Houdini willing us on, we finished 4th on the day and overhauled Croydon by 119 pts. to remain in the Premier Division.

23rd MARCH 1986 OSTEND 30th WELLINGTON INT. RELAY

52 teams again lined up for this popular event and a strong Blackheath contingent appeared to be likely winners. As usual we had a good blend of young (F.Ogwinda and Gavin Warr) and old (Stuart Paton!) with everyone producing creditable efforts. The one clang was down to one of our number who started to take his tracksuit off when the incoming runner was 60m away. The subsequent time loss may have cost us 3rd place, but nonetheless we still ran 24 sec. faster than last years bronze medal winning team. Our limited group of supporters: Jim Mote, John Piggott and Jo Laws were joined on the day of the race by the whole Coy family who travelled by boat for a rough 11 hours to provide welcome support. Greatly appreciated by all concerned.

6th APRIL 1986 WIMBLEDON BELGRAVE BROOKS 12 STAGE RELAY

A strong looking Belgrave team was assembled for our own 12 Stage again held on a Sunday.

Gary Staines had stepped into the breach on STAGE ONE but was only a shadow of himself as he finished 32nd. Gary has had a traumatic period in his young life and as usual gave 100%. His time looked bad in comparison to the other first leg runners, but actually it was quite good.

STAGE TWO saw Mark Sinclair in a determined mood and made up seven places. Next on STAGE THREE came Dave North and Dave winning Bels fastest long stage of the day gained nine places and we were up to 16th. STAGE FOUR Dave handed to Andy who flew around the course passing team after team and chasing Steve Karens and Seb Coe. Andy ran the fifth fastest time of the day and Bels were in 7th - our highest position for many years.

STAGE FIVE saw Frank Ward determined to hang on to as high a position as possible to enable John Gladwin to make an impact on STAGE SIX. John was in true form and ran the third fastest time of the day and Bels were back in fifth place. John looked a class apart and seems to have acquired a maturity to his undoubted talent. Olly Foote started STAGE SEVEN and the second half of the race and ran a solid leg to hand to young Glyn Tiernan in 7th place. For STAGE EIGHT Glyn ran a blinder and gave promise of a rich talent with a fine 16.47. Next on STAGE NINE came Mike Hayes with another good run to push us back up to sixth. STAGE TEN saw another newcomer to our team Richard O'Hara with a good stint to maintain our position. Richard handed over to his training companion, Marcello who ran really well to keep us in sixth place. Paul Richley ran STAGE TWELVE and showed that he is on his way back as he finished in sixth position, our best for many years from a very good team.

Results: 1.Shaftesbury 4:04.45 (Rec); 2.Luton 4:09.02; 3.AFD 4:12.19; 6.BELGRAVE 4:15.38.

BELS: Long: 1.G.Staines 26.25; 3.D.North 25.23; 5.F.Ward 27.01; 7.O.Foote 25.49; 9.M.Hayes 26.14; 11.M.Bizio 26.34

Short: 2.M.Sinclair 16.17; 4.A.North 15.42; 6.J.Gladwin 15.13; 8.G.Tiernan 16.47; 10.R.O'Hara 17.12; 12.P.Richley.
