7th SEPTEMBER 1986 WESTMINSTER MILE - RELAYS

Over the last few years televised mile spectaculars have become a must for capital cities. Westminster Council have taken over from the GLC in promoting London's premier road race and this year not only did John Gladwin get an invitation for the International race, but also our Seniors and Youths teams were invited to compete in the relays - and with good effect! John, fresh! from his participation in Cram's 1500m world record attempt 2 days earlier confounded all the pundits by outkicking a class field including John Walker, Jack Buckner, Tim Hutchings and Dave Moorcroft. To this we added a first in the mens team and a third in the Youths. Outstanding was Matthew Kinane's leg as he chased for second, producing a time only 5 seconds slower than that of Mark Sinclair in the Senior event. Justin Chaston made a welcome first appearance in our colours and should be a key part of future successes.

4x1.1m Youths: 1.Sale 19.49, 2.Stretford AC 20.06, 3.BELGRAVE 20.16, 4.Newham & Essex B. 20.38 ...7 teams.

Bels. Team: S.Webster 5.17, J.Chaston 5.02, G.Tiernan 5.02, M.Kinane 4.55 - Total 20.16

Report · page 45

Track and Field.

Reports by:- Bob Bridges, Leo Coy, Steve Williams and Bill Laws.

Track and Field.

Reports by:- Bob Bridges, Leo Coy, Steve Williams and Bill Laws.

Ernie Obeng was one of the first off the mark with a couple of fast 100m clockings in the USA - 10.58 in San Jose on 19 April and 10.22w in Walnut the following week.

* * *

SURREY TRIALS MEETING - TOOTING - 23 APRIL: Belgravians were active in all parts of the arena at this interesting new 'warm-up' meeting and all showed the promise of good things to come. None more so than John Gladwin in his seasonal debut, made over 3000m. Impressive hardly describes his 55 second final lap looking round for the opposition and a final time of 8.03.3! Results:- 60m 'B' 6 D Browne 7.6; 150m 'B' 4 D Williams 16.9 'C' 1 J Minetti 16.8; 600m 4 G Adams 1.22.3, 5 G Tiernan 1.23.6, 6 P Walker 1.24.2; 1000m 7 S Maynard 2.37.5; 3000m 1 J Gladwin 8.03.3, 7 D North 8.25.8, 10 A North 8.26.8, 11 O Foote 8.33.9, 16 A Gayton 9.06.1; 110mH 3 M Lindsay-Bayley 16.1; HJ 5 M Reece 1.85; TJ 3 D Browne 14.26 LJ 1 D Williams 7.25 SP 1 K Onuaguluchi 14.42, 2 A Ekoku 14.09, 5 M Small 12.07; DT K Onuaguluchi 51.80, 2 A Ekoku 46.44, 3 M Small 40.08; HT 4 M Small 45.28

* * * *

SCAAA v Lough v Civ Serv - 30/4:1500m 3 G Staines 3.48.51, 6 S Paton 3.53.85 SP 2 A Ekoku 14.51, 3 K Onuaguluchi 13.55 DT 1 K Onuaguluchi 53.70 *** SCAAA v WLIHE v RAF v Lon Univ - 7/5: 800m 1 J Gladwin 1.47.65 3000m 5 D North 8.22.6 3000mSc 2 M Hayes 9.04.57 *** AAA v WLIHE - 14/5: 100m 1 P Goedluck 11.0, 2 M Adam 11.0

At the Kent County Champ's at Crystal Palace on 18 May Tony Pamah was 2nd in the 400mH in 55.6.

In Oslo on 20 May, John Regis zipped to a legal pb 100m time of 10.49 and clocked 20.85 over 200m behind Linford Christie. And the following week, he was off to Italy to win over 200m in 21.10 in Florence. Also on 20 May, at the less exotic location of Southwark Park, 1st year Junior, Joe Minetti won the Southwark Open Championship 200m in a speedy 21.7.

UK CHAMPIONSHIPS - CWMBRAN - 25/26 MAY: John Regis was continually checking across at the opposition in the final 50m of the 200m and that's a measure of the ease with which he disposed of Todd Bennett and the rest to claim the title in 21.34 into a strong headwind. That was a great run from one Belgravian and what we hoped would be the first leg of a club double with John Gladwin going in the 1500m final along with Gary Staines. It was not to be but what a superb effort by John. He smashed his own club record with 3.36.28 only for a peak form Harrison to rob him of the title in the home straight. Gary took 5th in 3.45.01 while Stuart Paton had run 3.51.52 in the heats. In the 100m, Marcus Adam was a fine 4th in 10.72 with Phil Goedluck 8th in 10.90. Mark Sinclair was another to smash his previous best, as a sparkling 8.44.88 gave him 6th place in the 'chase with Mike Hayes also under 9 minutes in the heats. There were prominent places too for Phil Lovell, 5th with 4.80 (PV) and Derrick Williams, 7th with 7.14 (LJ).

SCAAA CHAMPIONSHIPS - C/PALACE - 6/7 JUNE: No winners this year but Mike Hayes must take the plaudits for his great Silver medal run in the chase, slicing a further 9 seconds off his best time; Ken Onuaguluchi was runner up for the third time in a row and Phil Lovell collected his fourth consecutive medal although still finding that 5.00 metre vault elusive. 100m 4 P Goedluck 10.79 (10.74sf-pb) 7 M Adam 10.82 (10.71sf); Sf 8 D Williams 11.15(10.94ht pb) E Roofe 11.07 ht. 200m M Adam 21.93ht 400m Sf 8 M Alleyne 49.95 (49.36ht); Hts J Busk 50.09, M Bazire 50.61, G Sagar 51.62 800m 4 G Staines 1.50.84; R O'Hara 1.54.30sf; P Walker 1.57.53ht 1500m Hts G Tiernan 3.57.32(pb), P Richley 4.02.- , J Barry 3.59.- 5000m 11 O Foote 14.24.78, 20 D North 15.07.62;Standards F Ward 15.22.52 3000mSc 2 M Hayes 8.49.69pb; Stand. M Bizio 10.00.26 400mH Hts A Pamah 56.03 LJ 5 D Williams 7.14 PV 3 P Lovell 4.80 TJ 9 D Browne 14.47pb SP 8 A Ekoku 14.99pb DT 2 K Onuaguluchi 54.36, 5 A Ekoku 49.10

* * * *

Gothenburg 12 June: J Regis 21.31 Scottish Championships-14 June:1500m 8th S Paton 3.49.55 Loughborough v AAA-14 June:100m 1st E Obeng(L) 10.67 3rd M Adam(AAA) 10.94; 1500m 2nd G Staines (AAA) 3.40.25

Phil Goedluck celebrated his International debut in the Junior match against Oceania and Switzerland on 28 June by winning the 200m in 21.59.

England v USA-Gateshead-27 June: 100m 6th E Obeng 10.70; 200m 5th J Regis 21.38; 800m 2nd J Gladwin 1.46.34 Ulster Games - 30 June: 200m 3rd J Regis 21.14; Mile 14th G Staines (pacemaker) 4.03.04 Stockholm/Helsinki Grands Prix - 1/7 July E Obeng 100m 5/5 10.67/10.58 Inter Counties - Corby - 5 July: 400mH A Pamah 54.9ht Peugeot Talbot Games Crystal Palace 11 July: 100m 4th E Obeng 10.44 200m 6th J Regis 21.42 Mile 13th G Staines 4.00.00

BANK OF ENGLAND INVITATION MEETING - ROEHAMPTON - 16 JULY: 100m A 3 J Busk 11.2; B 4 A Paton 11.9 200m A 3 J Busk 23.2 B 4 P Walker 24.6 400m A 5 J Booth 53.2 B 1 G Adams 53.5 800m A 1 S Paton 1.55.0 B 1 M Hayes 2.02.6 (n/s J Chaston 2.01.7, A North 2.06.4, A Gayton 2.13.7) 1500m A 2 J Chaston 4.07.4 B 1 M Hayes 4.09.4 (n/s S Maynard 4.12.0, M Bizio 4.20.0, S Webster 4.22.8) 5000m A 2 J Wright 15.06.0 B 1 A McIntosh 15.24.0 4x400m 1 (R O'Hara, G Tiernan, G Adams, S Paton) 3.27.2 (n/s 3.31.6)Team 5th 75 pts.(Guy Sagar competing for the Bank did 11.9(100m) 2.70(LJ) & 34.20(JT)

* * * * *

At the Pearl Assurance International meeting held in Birmingham on 19 July, John Gladwin improved the club 800m record to 1.46.09 behind Steve Cram, having fought off 1.42 man Koskei down the home straight. Then in Paris 3 days later he improved still further, to a classy 1.45.14. At the Birmingham event Ernie Obeng ran 10.49 over 100m.

* * * * *

At the inaugural World Junior Championships held in Athens from 16-20 July, Philip Goedluck proved himself to be one of the best in the World at his age when taking a fine 5th place in the 200m final in 21.38. Such was the competition that 2 rounds and semis were necessary and held on the same day. It is a tribute to Phil and his coach that he came up with his best form to date for this big event. All four of his times were faster than his previous legal best- 21.36ht 21.25 Rd2 & 21.32sf. Phil actually beat the eventual Bronze medallist in one round so it was a pity he missed out on an individual medal. However some compensation was in store when he contributed a great backstraight leg to the winning UK 4x100m effort which equalled the UK Junior record (you'll remember John Regis helped set it last year). But it was a good job Phil had a long arm to stretch to the flying Kirton for their changeover; every hundredth counted at the end with 3 teams hardly separable by the human eye.

* * * * *

At the AAA U/20 Championships in Birmingham on 9/10 August, Marcus Adam and Phil Goedluck gave further proof of their status as the most exciting junior sprint prospects in the country with double medal winning performances. Well done to both, particularly Marcus on this occasion for getting the win in the 200m. If only he could have been at the World Juniors too! Marcus may be interested to know that a Belgravian already has his name on this trophy for Alan Sexton twice won the then 220 yards title in 1948 and 1949, his 22.1 on the latter occasion constituting a AAA Junior record. Perhaps Marcus will emulate that feat next season! Results:- 100m 2nd M Adam 10.63; 3rd P Goedluck 10.78 200m 1st M Adam 21.09; 2nd P Goedluck 21.21

* * * * *

U/20 Inter Counties Match - 3 August 200m 1st M Adam 21.2; 2000mSc 1st S Maynard 6.01.4 *** IAC Int.3 Aug 100m 5th E Obeng 10.45; 800m 5th J Gladwin 1.46.12:*** Budapest 11 Aug E Obeng 10.45ht SCAAA Match 6 Aug-1500m 3rd S Paton 3.49.85; 3000m 3rd M Sinclair 8.12.01; 3000mSc 6th M Hayes 9.23.77; PV 1st P Lovell 4.80; TJ 4th D Browne 14.64; SP 3rd A Ekoku 14.23; DT 1st K Onuaguluchi 54.44:*** Zurich 13 Aug B100m 4th E Obeng 10.55; B800m 4th J Gladwin 1.45.72 *** SCAAA Match 13 Aug 1500m 3rd S Paton 3.52.7; 3000m 4th K Sturman 8.15.9; PV 3rd P Lovell 4.60; TJ 3rd D Browne 14.05; HT 5th M Small 47.86.*** Marcus Adam produced a very fine sprint double for GB Juniors v WG Juniors in Gottingen on 16 Aug with 10.82 and 21.10, while Phil Goedluck nearly did the same in a Home Junior International at Meadowbank the same day! 100/200 2/1 10.6w/21.28w

* * * * *

At the Dairy Crest Games in Birmingham on 19 August, Ernie Obeng (10.46 3rd in the BR Sprinter Trophy) John Regis (20.98) and Gary Staines (3.59.97 Mile) returned to something nearer their best form while Marcus and Phil took 2nd and 3rd in the BR Junior Sprinter Trophy final. But the special mention on this occasion goes to Mark Sinclair who mixed it with some of the best men in the World in a 2000mSc, finishing not too far behind the Commonwealth Champion in a time of 5.33.59, and only just shy of John Bicourt's club best set in the year John ran his 8.22 !

JOHN GLADWIN'S Commonwealth games performance and his consistently good record over the season gave him a further major Games selection for Stuttgart and the 14th European Championships held at the end of August. John qualified for the final by winning his heat in a fast but comfortable 3.36.85 and based on that run and the efforts of the other qualifiers, had much more than an outside chance of a medal despite the presence of Cram and Coe. So when John was run into 5th place (3.42.57) after a very fast last lap - John himself ran it in 53.0 - he was somewhat disappointed. But then John set about getting in some fast races to finish the season and he showed himself to still be in the tremendous form he has exhibited since April by setting further club records for 1500m in Brussels - 3.35.26 - and the Mile at Crystal Palace - 3.54.52. Congratulations John on a marvellous season.

SCAAA Matches:- 31.8 5000m 4th K Sturman 14.28.0; 14.9 200m 1st P Goedluck 21.6 At a meeting to open a new track in Staffs, Phil Lovell again equalled the club Pole Vault record with 4.90.** That elegant stylist, Kevin Sturman ran one of the fastest ever 10000m times by a Belgravian (29.48.3) in taking 6th place in the Southern Championships on 10 Sept.** John Regis took 3rd place behind Wells and Christie over 100m at Crystal Palace on 12 Sept, John's time of 10.43 was another pb and one hopes to see him competing more over this distance in future as a complement to his 200m running.

Three centimetres separated Abi Ekoku from the club shot record at Crystal Palace on 27 September when he put a mighty 15.75 to win at the Croydon Field and Hurdles meeting. This record has stood since 1959 and at long last looks ready for revision next season. At the same meeting Ken won the Discus, Mike Small took third in the Hammer with 49.06 and Marcello Bizio recorded a steeplechase pb.

STOP PRESS??

ABI EKOKU has since raised the Club Shot Putt record to a mighter 15.94 metres

J.G.C. Edt

SURREY COUNTY CHAMPIONSHIPS - 26/27 APRIL - 17 MAY 1986

WRL

SURREY COUNTY CHAMPIONSHIPS - 26/27 APRIL - 17 MAY 1986

It's not every day that a County Championship record is broken. This year our senior men broke 5 with John Gladwin setting new records in both the 800m and 1500m within the space of 3 hours. His efforts erased times set as long ago as 1971 and 1969 and provided an early season glimpse of the results of a solid Winter's training. Mark Sinclair's steeplechase season got underway with his CBP, while in the Discus, Ken's new CBP was almost overshadowed by Abi's breakthrough into +50 meter throwing. Mike Small threw a pb in the Hammer and is nudging the Club record. But top honours go to our 800m men who placed 4 in the first 5 with Richard O'Hara taking a big slice off his pb as too did new member Phil Walker. Good efforts by the Juniors with a fistfull of medals but where oh where were the Colts and Boys?

WRL

Report · page 47

AAA CHAMPIONSHIPS - CRYSTAL PALACE - 20/21 JUNE 1986 WORLD CLASS PERFORMANCES FROM THE TWO JOHNS

AAA CHAMPIONSHIPS - CRYSTAL PALACE - 20/21 JUNE 1986 WORLD CLASS PERFORMANCES FROM THE TWO JOHNS

We'd waited a long time between AAA track titles before Ernie's victory last year - but now we had 2 Champions in the space of 2 hours!

At about 3.30 on Saturday afternoon, John Regis lined up alongside Linford Christie, Todd Bennett, Ade Mafe et al for the 200m final; 20.41 seconds later he was punching the air in celebration of a superb win. Christie, drawn in the lane inside John was first into top gear and he led our man by a metre coming off the bend with the rest in a line just behind But that wasn't enough for the UK 100m record holder. John powered down the straight and turned that deficit into a metre advantage with a supreme demonstration of aggressive but relaxed sprinting. Up went both arms before the line and that ample chest, resplendent in club colours, stopped the electronic timer in the third fastest ever time by a Briton, a UK teenage best and, very obviously, a club record. Quite magnificent! Commonwealth selection was ensured and at the end of the afternoon, John received the Mumm Champagne award for the best performance of the afternoon from none other than our own Arthur Penny.

Of course that decision must have been difficult for any Belgravian to make as later on there was a special 1500m!

John Gladwin needed to avenge that 'UK' defeat by Harrison, and win, to be sure of a Commonwealth spot, while Gary Staines, with a new pb from the week before, could also look for a prominent showing. The early pace was respectable with Gary taking it up at about 680m and hitting the 2 lap mark in 1.57.6. But John had been biding his time on the outside of the bunch and with 600m left he took off for home. The "Cram type approach" worked to perfection. Nobody could go with him (not surprising when you consider his closing speed - the last 3x200 in 28.3, 27.9 and 27.2 successively!) and he came home nearly 20 metres to the good in 3.35.93, a new Championship Best Performance and John's umpteenth club record. This was the 106th running of the event and there can have been few more dominant displays.

No finer compliment can be paid to our two than to list a handful of previous winners of these titles. 220yds/200m - Eric Liddell, McDonald Bailey, Steve Riddick, Don Quarrie, Mel Lattany and Stanley Floyd. Mile/1500m - Jack Lovelock, Sydney Wooderson, Roger Bannister, Eamonn Coghlan, Dave Moorcroft, Steve Ovett and Steve Cram. Results: 200m 1 J Regis 20.41; 2 T Bennett 20.50; 3 L Christie 20.51 1500m 1 J Gladwin 3.35.93; 2 S Crabb 3.38.04; 3 P Larkins 3.38.19 The two Johns then were the stars of the weekend but others were competing with success as well. Phil Goedluck and Marcus Adam were flying in the sprints. Phil was in great form with pb's of 10.55 and 10.48w in the 100m heats and semis and 21.22w in the 200m heats, close up with those of bigger reputation, while Marcus was near his best with a 21.55w heat. Both men later qualified from the first semi final of the British Rail Sprinter Trophy for Juniors over 100m, Marcus getting the jump on Phil 10.53 to 10.67, as they took 2nd and 3rd places. In the Triple Jump, Derek Browne bounded out to another pb of 14.77, the first time he had used a 15 stride approach instead of 9, and that obviously worked, while Mike Hayes was a creditable 9th in the steeplechase with 8.53.16.

The rest of our lads were perhaps not quite up to scratch and did not perform as we know they can. Ernie Obeng won his 100m heat in 10.45w but missed out on progress from the semis by the thickness of a vest. Also Mike Alleyne, 49.79 400m ht; Phil Lovell 4.70 and 11th place (PV); Derrick Williams, three fouls after a 7.19w Long Jump qualifier; Abi Ekoku, 17th in the Discus with 45.50; Mark Sinclair, retiring from the 'chase after starting with a heavy cold and then cracking a knee badly on a barrier. And, rounding off that 1500m, Gary, who would have hoped for something better than his 8th place in 3.45.29 after his promising early laps.

Report · page 49

XIII COMMONWEALTH GAMES - EDINBURGH - 24 JULY/2 AUGUST 1986

XIII COMMONWEALTH GAMES - EDINBURGH - 24 JULY/2 AUGUST 1986

"Second, and the winner of the Silver Medal - John Gladwin, representing England"

No doubt our 23 year old club record holder will long remember those words from the medal presentation ceremony, along with his remarkable performance in the 1500 metres final........... A slow handclap greeted the opening lap when nobody was willing to take it on on a very windy day so some people present couldn't have realised this wasn't a Grand Prix meeting! 800 metres passed in 2.15 - maybe that wouldn't distress too many in the Rosenheim League but every second was absorbing - and in the next 50 metres John jostled his way to the outside in order to execute a pre race plan to run for home 500 metres out. Just before the home straight he took off. Giving it everything into the bell and round to the backstraight - a 200m he covered in 24.6! - John opened up a gap that was scarcely believable in this class of field. He really had taken everyone by surprise and for a few moments it looked like he would even do the impossible and beat Cram. It wasn't to be though as with 160m left, the World No.1, who later admitted to being a worried man, finally caught John. But our man wasn't letting his hard work count for nothing and he kept hard on Cram's heels for a magnificent Silver medal.

Taking a place on the lap of honour John had to survive an almighty embrace from Keith Sinclair before later receiving his medal from The Duke of Edinburgh and then enjoying the closing ceremony celebrations. That evening, at an alcoholic establishment close by the Games Village, John rounded off the day in company with a group of friends from Belgrave and Lauriston, the imbibing going on into the wee small hours! 1st S Cram 3.50.87 2nd John Gladwin 3.52.17 3rd D Campbell (Can) 3.54.06

Earlier in the week we had seen John Regis progress fairly comfortably to the 200m final (3rd ht 21.26, 2nd sf 21.23) although seemingly not quite firing on all cylinders and, for the semis and final, he had his left thigh heavily strapped as a precautionary matter. John took 8th place in the final (21.08) behind some of the best sprinters in the World, and, if that wasn't John's top form, it was still a fine performance for a teenager who can look forward to further Games selections in the years ahead. Ernie Obeng, who was looking forward to his third Games was unfortunately kept out of the England 4x100 squad by the good form of others.

John GladwinS CramKeith SinclairThe Duke of EdinburghD CampbellJohn RegisErnie Obeng

Report · page 51

GRE/BRITISH LEAGUE DIVISION II 1986

GRE/BRITISH LEAGUE DIVISION II 1986

10th MAY 1986 WEST LONDON MATCH 1

Our 1986 British League campaign got off to a flying start at West London on a damp and very windy day. TVH got a clear lead and held it throughout the afternoon, but gradually the 'Belgrave Machine' roared into action and with the relays to come, we were one point ahead. The 4x100 team sealed our victory and the 4x400 made certain. Highlights of our win included 12 winners. Simon Maynard and Abi Ekoku won on their BAL debuts. John Regis and Marcus Adam in his debut put the frighteners on Linford Christie. John Gladwin cruised round the 1500 and was chased home by John Barry. Stuart Paton, Derrick William, Ken, Phil Goedluck, David North all won their events with good performances. Phil Lovell won the vault on his last attempt at 4.60 (phew!). A great start to the season!

31st MAY 1986 CWMBRAN MATCH 2

A large contingent travelled to Wales for our Second Round Match. Our supporters were in good voice and got noisier as we scored a comfortable victory. TVH lost ground and Cardiff did us a favour by finishing 3rd. The Scottish Challenge never materialised and it was left to Southampton to finish 2nd on the day. Twelve wins were again recorded and included Gary Staines running away from Dave Baptiste, Phil Goedluck, Mark Sinclair, Derrick Williams (Long and Triple), Ken and Abi in the Discus, Phil Lovell and Mike Hayes and Simon Maynard in the Chase. John Regis was beaten on the dip by Colin Jackson and Todd Bennett in the 100 and Marcus was given the same treatment in the 200. But the 4x100 recorded a magnificent win in 41.26. Abi beat Ken in the Shot by 1cm. Derek Browne continued his fine improvement in the Triple. Mike Alleyne and Dave Sheldon recorded p.b's. Richard O'Hara and Phil McKay made their debuts as also did Jay Busk who was the star of the day with his win in the 400 B, and recording a p.b. 48.85.

At the end of two matches we were top of the league by three clear points.

5th JULY 1986 MEADOWBANK MATCH 3

Most of the team were air lifted by B.Cal to Edinburgh for the third match, in the impressive Meadowbank Stadium. Edinburgh Southern fielded their strongest team and won comfortably. We managed a safe second place with the other four teams finishing with just four points between them. Star of the day was Ernest Obeng who broke Alan Wells' League Record in the 100 with 10.39. Marcus made it a double by winning the 'B', and also the 200 'B'. The short relay was another fine win with Mike Alleyne taking the injured Derrick Williams place. Mike Small finished 2nd in the Hammer behind Chris Black. Ken and Abi again won the Discus and Phil Lovell easily won the Vault with another fine display. In the Shot Abi was 2nd in the 'A' just ahead of Ken who won the 'B'. Vince recorded his best throw of the season in the Javelin.

So, after three matches promotion to Division 1 was almost assured. Mark Lindsay-Bayley organised the evening meal, and Belgrave took over Dario's Restaurant for the evening. A quick dash was made across Edinburgh to see John Gladwin on T.V. A good weekend was had by all, except TVH.

23rd AUGUST 1986 BATTERSEA PARK MATCH 4

The final match for this season and again we turned out a strong team. TVH had to beat Southampton by two places to gain promotion. They did beat Southampton, but not by enough. So, Southampton will join us in Division 1 next year.

Mike Small got off to a great start by finishing second in the Hammer with 49.50, ably backed by Terry Welsh. Phil Lovell again won the Vault, and Abi and Ken won the Discus. Derek Browne improving all season scored a great win in the Triple, and he was backed by Abi! Derrick Williams made a sensational debut in the 400H, as did Chris Millbank in his first BAL S/C. Not content to do a p.b. Chris had a rest at the last barrier! Just behind him Simon Maynard had another great run and a p.b. Kev Sturman ran the 1500 and was unlucky to finish 4th. Abi recorded a p.b. of 15.51 in the Shot.

Our four top sprinters were all competing with ernest just pipped by Nigel Walker in the 100 and John, Marcus and Phil all winning their events. The sprinters combined to form a powerful squad in the 4x100, but shaky changes ruled out a very fast time, but still equalling the club record.

Mark Sinclair ran a brilliant 5000, with the best time by a club athlete for many years. Star of the day was John Gladwin, who on the eve of his departure to the Europeans, smashed the league record for the 800. The 4x400 squad also ran the fastest metric time ever recorded by Belgrave.

Battersea Park looked splendid thanks to great work by Clive Shippen, Charlie Manning and Wandsworth Council. It was a fitting end to a great season.

Report · page 52

Round Up SOUTHERN LEAGUE DIVISION 2

Bob Bridges

Round Up SOUTHERN LEAGUE DIVISION 2

I will start by thanking all of those who turned out this year, be it to compete or officiate, thus making it an enjoyable and successful season. Once again we saw an improvement in our performances over the previous year, this time finishing 6th in Division 2 and missing promotion by 1 league point with the top 5 going up.

It is not only our individual performances that are up - our team spirit is riding high. I think this is shown by our commitment in the long relay which we won 5 times out of 6. It is not only the British League that provides entertainment and excitement; you should have heard the cheer which greeted our last victory by one point on the cinders at Guildford.

Having said that, a look through the results will show that we were still missing a few competitors in the less 'popular' events and don't forget that at every match there is one sprint and one middle distance non-scoring event. So do come along and enjoy yourself, be it to partake or spectate. The Southern League is proving to be a good 'feeder' for the British League and if we continue to make progress as we have, we should gain promotion into Division 1 next season, our centenary year.

Bob Bridges

Match Reports

The first match at Battersea saw us successful by a healthy margin of 16.5 points from the next club with our superiority over local rivals SLH being particularly noteworthy. Admittedly a few of our 'A' team men were on view, with John Barry scoring an easy 1500 win and Abi Ekoku giving us maximum points in the Shot and Discus, but we had no 'B' competitor in two events and the selected 'B' 1500m runner didn't turn up so we were still giving away points unnecessarily. Personal bests were set by Joe Minetti (100) Jay Busk (400) Richard O'Hara (800) Simon Maynard (5000) and Marcello Bizio ('chase) while those two stalwarts John Bicourt and Snowy Brooks gave quite a few years and a beating to most of their rivals.

We had a few defectors for the match on cinders at Stevenage but it didn't make much difference; we took 2nd place. The throwers again excelled with Mike Small scoring 15 points and Snowy Brooks was only a metre or so shy of his best ever Discus throw set some 8 years ago. Again we took the 'A' races from 100m through 1500m and at the halfway stage in the season we were in 4th place in the division behind Woking, Oxford and Bedford.

The match at Brighton gave us an even bigger win with 18 individual firsts. The throwers were in good form with Neil Phillips winning 3 events and Tony Featherstone 2. Roy Vassall came up with his best Long Jump for a while and also improved in the Pole Vault reportedly using the pole climbing technique that was banned by the AAA in 1920! Nice one Roy. Mike Bazire notched a sprint double and Jay Busk dipped under 50 seconds in the 400m for the first time. Debutants Phil Walker and Chris Knight won impressively in their respective events while Paul Richley found some of his old sub 4 form in the 1500m, with Graham Adams running a pb just behind.

The bubble eventually burst at Havering in July when we could only scrape up 4th place with a team containing too many people in non specialist events. Even so we had some winners with Stu' Paton maintaining our 400m 'A' record and helping us to victory in the long relay. Chris Knight continued to do a good job in the low hurdles with good back up from Gareth Allison who found he could high hurdle as well and competed in 5 events during the afternoon. Kevin McCallion, a new Irish Guards member won the Pole Vault.

The match at Portsmouth proved to be a Navy benefit day and we lost further ground in the league. Stu' Paton continued to strengthen our team and again he anchored the 4x4 effort in fine style. Andy Moore came in for his first race of the season and proved his good form with Richard O'Hara too strong as a 'B' man in this class of competition. Jim Gregg brought repectability to our High Jumping and Gareth Allison set a club Youth's record in the 400mH. The whole match was of very high standard which also saw Roy Vassall finish only 4th in the Long Jump with 6.61, a distance good enough to win most other matches. --- Continued at bottom of P. 52.

Continued from the Southern League Report on P.54.

We returned to winning ways at Guildford but it was just too late for our promotion hopes. Still, Chris Knight showed the sort of form that could see him knocking on the door for BAL selection next year while the improving Chris Millbank upset the form book by taking the 'A' scoring spot away from John Bicourt in the 'chase. Aham Amadi made a welcome return after injury and helped us to a 'Triple' double with John Druce. Our Javelin pair also scored a maximum and we believe Guy Sagar would be even better with a 400m run up! This match also saw Errol Roofe make his Southern League debut for us and although 'not fit' he managed to win the 100m 'A' and help the short relay to victory. The highlight of the match and perhaps the season was the 4x400m relay which we had to win and did to take the match by 1 point from our friendly hosts, Guildford.

Results · page 55

ROSENHEIM LEAGUE 1986

ROSENHEIM LEAGUE 1986

For the first time this year, the final was held on an 8 lane track and so only one team from each division needed elimination through the five league matches. Surrey Beagles were the unlucky ones from our division. The overall standard in the final was possibly the best ever and despite a reasonable Senior team we could only finish 4th.

Needless to say it was the 'A' team members who turned out through the season who provided the best quality performances and mention must once again be made of the support and efforts of Mike Small. However tucked away in the results you'll find confirmation of the improvement of many of our younger members with the fine 3000m times of Juniors, Graham Adams and Simon Maynard, and Youth, Steve Webster - a new club record - the most notable. You'll also notice the return to Belgrave of Eric Barker, one of our best ever throwers, and of Alan McIntosh, our double cross country champion from 1979/80.

Note for next year:- If you think you might be needed as a scorer in any event, please turn up as early as you can to assist the team manager with selections. We might not take the league that seriously but we might as well do our best!

Column · page 59

Veterans.

Veterans.

EVERGREEN SNOWY BROOKS SETS NEW WORLD BEST

Snowy achieved a new World Record in the B.V.A.F. Indoor Pentathlon at Cosford on March 15th, 1986.

In the over 40 years section he recorded 2749 pts beating the previous best of 2573 pts by Bill Smith (U.S.A.). Snowy's individual events performances were:

60m Hurdles: 9.3 secs. L.J: 5.77m Shot: 11.74m HJ: 1.63m 1000m: 3:12.2

Congratulations Snowy.

Report · page 60

The Ladies.

Harry Porter

The Ladies.

After the euphoria of gaining promotion to Division 2 of the Southern League in September, the Winter Season was rather an anti-climax. Over the country in the Surrey Womens League the seniors were unable to repeat the result of the previous season although we had a few more turning out, but injuries and illness to some runners meant that we were short at times. Having said that the Juniors finished in 3rd place after the 4 races and the Inters, who were having a separate race for the first time were 5th. The Seniors were 7th and the Girls 10th. In the County Championships we only had 2 representatives in the Middlesex and our Surrey team was depleted with no Senior representative. In the Juniors Jacqui Smiter was 13th and Natalie Fenyoe (Middlesex) was 17th Girl home.

A new name appeared on the Senior Club Cross Country cup. Well done to Vicky Mitchell and another win in the Juniors for Jackie Stone.

Our Road Runners have been strengthened by the addition of Frances Guy, who broke several existing records quite considerably and was 2nd in the Mitcham A.C. 25Km Road Race and 2nd in the St. Albans Marathon. Meanwhile and I won't let on about her age, but Pam Davies keeps on going.

Our walkers too put in some good times with Rachael Parrott and Charlotte Christou in the Juniors and some excellent Walking from Susan Kew as well as Gail Porter (3rd in the Enfield Open 7 Miles).

During the winter season Wendy Dunsford's shot putting had improved considerably indoors and was putting over 12 metres. Team Selection was going to be difficult as we had 5 Senior shot putters all over 10 metres.

Turning to the summer season we started off very slowly and after our 3rd Match found ourselves in 23rd place in the table. Unfortunately we went down to Cambourne with a good team, but the match was rained off. We won our last 2 fixtures though and finished 9th. For the first time and as an experiment, we had a 2nd team and they finished 15th. Also we got through to the Semi Final of the GRE Cup for the first time and although we were 7th there were 3 club records that afternoon. We were also invited by Adidas to an International Athletes Club Field Events Championship at Birmingham where we finished 4th.

In the Thameside League the girls and juniors did well also and over all we won the Midweek League again.

The experiment of running a 2nd Southern League team was reasonably successful and will be continued for next season.

We had 4 County Championship winners - Wendy Dunsford (Middlesex-Shot), Helen Kemp (Surrey-Shot), Joanne Hilliard (Kent-High Jump) and Charlotte Christou (Middlesex 2500m Walk) all these plus Cindy James (Silver Medal in the Middlesex Shot) represented their county's in the Inter County Championships. In the Southern Championships Joanne Hilliard was our only medal winner coming 3rd in the High Jump.

On the roads Frances Guy continued her winning ways with firsts in the Edinburgh Marathon, the Poly Marathon and the Belgrave 20 Mile Road Race.

Well done to everybody for a good summer season, but we look forward to promotion to Division 1 and Division 3 in our Centenary Year (1987).

Thanks to all those people who came along to help and support (officials, parents etc.) and all in all with good team work from all quarters of the section without which we would find it very difficult. So, once again, thanks to all those concerned. Good Luck -

Harry Porter

Jacqui SmiterNatalie FenyoeVicky MitchellJackie StoneFrances GuyPam DaviesRachael ParrottCharlotte ChristouSusan KewGail PorterWendy DunsfordHelen KempJoanne HilliardCindy JamesHarry Porter

Column · page 75

MISCELLANEOUS

English Schools - Portsmouth - 11/12 July: Snr Boys TJ 4th D Browne 14.93w/14.86; 800m G Adams 1.54.9ht; Inter Boys 800 A Ross 2.08.2ht

* * * * *

National Vets Champs - Wigan - 12/13 July: Pent 1st C Brooks 3143 (5.95,35.80,25.5, 33.96,5.18.2) At an England Junior match in Belgium on 13 July Marcus Adam tied for 3rd place over 100 in 10.62.

* * * * *

SCAAA U/20 Champs - C/Palace 19/20 July: Jnrs - 100 J Minetti 11.21ht 200 6th J Minetti 22.39 2000mSc 5th S Maynard 6.01.44; Boys - 100 I Gilley 11.92 (club rec) 800 5th A Ross 2.04.89 (club rec)

* * * * *

There was further success for Snowy Brooks at the European Vets Championships in Malmo, Sweden at the end of July with Bronze medals in the 110mH (17.01;16.94ht)and the Pentathlon 3471 pts (6.25, 46.44, 25.23, 36.52, 5.25.80). Although missing a medal in the Long Jump by one place, Snowy's effort of 6.59 was pretty useful even if wind assisted.

D BrowneG AdamsA RossC BrooksMarcus AdamJ MinettiS MaynardI Gilley

The Belgravian was Belgrave Harriers’ club gazette (1925–2009). Scans by Alan Mead, club historian (belgraveharriers.info). Text transcribed exactly as printed; from 1976 onwards private addresses and phone numbers are withheld. Spotted a mistake, or want an entry removed? Email belgravian@belgraveharriers.com.
